# F-SNP preparation: the tenant-snapshot dataset pipeline (isolated, CPU-only)

Lane record for the F-SNP preparation slice on `sprint/l23-fsnp` (worktree `~/dev/erev-wt/l23`, branched from main 8d76fea while main is frozen; merged later when the supervisor lifts the freeze). Dispatched by the supervisor after Codex's `PRODUCTION-CRITICAL-PATH-AUDIT-8d76fea.md` §3 (F-SNP needs explicit isolated preparation ownership). Binding: BUILD_SPEC SNP-1 to SNP-3 (`docs/BUILD_SPEC.md:9416`, `:9443`, `:9469`), 05 §10 SBX-01 to SBX-11 (`docs/05-ARCHITECTURE.md:1420-1430`), 05 §6.17 PRV-06 (`:1217`), 04 T-PLT-34 (`docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md:1623`), T-PLT-01 (`source_tenant_id`, `source_known_at`), T-PLT-29 `file_object`, §14.1 DB-15 (`:4941`), 03 REQ-PLT-022 to -025 (`docs/03-REQUIREMENTS.md:189-192`), PRD ACT-50 (`docs/02-PRD.md:1869`: permission `tenant.snapshot`, step-up required), `.run/supervisor/d91/lane-dispatch-common.md` with its amendments (13:56 diagnostic output, 14:04 one chain, 14:17 worktree lock, 16:08 targeted DB runs). Status vocabulary: every item below is **committed (lane)**; nothing is merged, gate-measured on main, or a completed snapshot feature.

## 1. Corrections the audit requires (recorded first)

- **Prerequisites are real.** The brief `lanes/F-SNP.md:16` reads "None hard"; the authoritative SNP-1 body (`BUILD_SPEC.md:9417`) requires **GATE-RPS** (file-store encryption PRV-06 and `platform_session` from **GATE-PLF**), and the readiness matrix row `:371` keeps that dependency with GATE-RPS still open (`:522`). This lane prepares pure modules and plans only; it asserts no snapshot feature "complete", ticks no BUILD_SPEC item and asserts no migration priority. SNP-1's acceptance (T-PLT-34 revision, `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` handler, `POST /tenant/snapshots`, the `test_snapshots.py` and `test_tenant_snapshots.py` tests) stays open until the gates are met and the integration lane runs on a provisioned database.
- **Databases.** The lane databases `erev_rv_l23_*` are named in the lane's `.env` but are **not provisioned** (DG-FORBID-03: Ray-side); every DB-bound item below is "not run — databases not provisioned". Ports 8260 / 5340 (e2e 8269 / 5349) were verified free; the dev DB `erev` and ports 8190 / 5270 are never touched.

## 2. Scope table

| Area | This lane (pure, CPU-only) | Not this lane (integration after GATE-RPS / GATE-PLF, on a provisioned DB) |
|---|---|---|
| Inventory of snapshot datasets | Every table of `erev_api.db.tables.metadata` classified exactly once: copied (facts, configuration, replay reference) or excluded with a reason (derived, audit, credential/session, integration, support, operational, global reference, snapshot bookkeeping), per SBX-03; a drift guard fails when a new table is unclassified | Reading the rows (SBX-04 repeatable-read batches) |
| JSONL encoding | One canonical JSON object per row (DG-KRN-CAN-01 to -15 through `erev_engine.canonical`), rows in a deterministic order (primary key; `record_seq` for streams), LF-terminated, UTF-8; decoder; round trip | Writing the files to the file store as `SNAPSHOT_DATASET` (`files.store.put_file`, PRV-06 encryption, storage key `<tenant>/<purpose>/<sha256>`) |
| Content hashes and row counts | SHA-256 of the plaintext JSONL bytes (T-PLT-29 `sha256` semantics); row count = line count; manifest = canonical JSON of the dataset entries + `known_at`, source tenant, purpose, exclusions; `manifest_sha256` = SHA-256 of the manifest bytes (T-PLT-34) | T-PLT-34 row, `manifest_file_id`, `row_counts`, `status` transitions (DB-03) |
| `known_at` stamping and cutoff | Per-table cutoff rule: `contract_event` by `recorded_at ≤ known_at` (SBX-03), other copied tables by `created_at ≤ known_at`, none for tables without a timestamp; the stamp travels in the manifest | `tenant.source_known_at` on the sandbox row (T-PLT-01) |
| Exclusion rules | The immutable source is never altered: the pipeline only reads; a `source_untouched(before, after)` comparison of row counts (SBX-11) and the excluded populations (sessions, API clients and tokens, MFA factors, recovery codes, password resets, security events, audit events and chain heads, integration connections, webhook endpoints and deliveries, support grants, jobs, notifications, saved views, idempotency records, derived engine and journal tables, report runs, evidence packs, lock and disclosure snapshots, `tenant_snapshot`); binary (ciphertext) columns are refused by the codec | RLS proof (`test_sandbox_isolation.py`), DB-15 triggers |
| Sandbox load plan (SNP-2) | An explicit, tested table load order (parents before children), the step list of SBX-04 (provisioning-scope tenant of kind `sandbox` with `source_tenant_id` / `source_known_at`; streams in `record_seq` order, new `recorded_at` / `record_seq` by DB-08; all periods `open`; recompute every group with trigger `MIGRATION`; replay period states and locks through the normal transitions; `derived_mismatches`) and the gates each step needs (permission `tenant.snapshot`, fresh TOTP step-up, `app.platform_scope = 'provisioning'`) as data | `load_sandbox`, the job, `POST /tenant/sandboxes`, the determinism verification against real versions |
| Sandbox reset plan (SNP-3) | SBX-07 supersession as a step list: new sandbox from the chosen snapshot (or empty), old sandbox `status = ARCHIVED`, `scenario.scenario_tenant_id` repointed, the session moved; no row deleted | `SANDBOX_RESET` handler, `POST /tenant/reset` (04 API-R-04; BUILD_SPEC SNP-3), CTL-043 test (SNP-4) |

| `backend/erev_api/domain/platform/snapshot_dataset.py` (new; pure: standard library, `erev_engine.canonical`, `erev_api.db.tables` metadata only) | `classify(metadata) -> Inventory` (every table → `Dataset(name, category, cutoff_column, order_key)` or `Exclusion(name, reason)`); `encode_rows(dataset, rows) -> Encoded(name, content, sha256, row_count)`; `decode_rows(content)`; `cutoff(dataset, rows, known_at)`; `Manifest`, `manifest_bytes`, `manifest_sha256`, `verify_manifest(manifest, files)`; `source_untouched(before, after)`; `LOAD_ORDER` and `load_plan(purpose)` / `reset_plan()` step lists with their gates | `backend/tests/unit/test_snapshot_dataset.py` (committed there, not under `tests/domain/platform/`): inventory completeness and SBX-03 membership; Hypothesis round trip (encode → decode → encode byte-identical; order-independence; count = lines; hash stability; float and bytes refused; timezone-naive datetimes refused); cutoff monotonicity; manifest hash and verification; load-order parent-before-child; plan gates |
| (later slices, same lane or the integration lane) `backend/erev_api/domain/platform/sandboxes.py` | `create_snapshot`, `export_snapshot(jc, params)` writing the datasets through `put_file`; `load_sandbox`; `reset_sandbox` | SNP-1 to SNP-3 acceptance tests (DB) |

Naming and shared files: the F-RPS / ENG-E1 lane (`sprint/l17`, lane-eng-b1) owns the **lock-snapshot** registry `domain/reports/snapshots.py::SNAPSHOT_DATASETS` (twelve E-64 report kinds, S15-R-18 CSV, T-CLS-05; `F-RPS-ENG-E1-prep.md` §8) — a different object from the **tenant snapshot** (one JSONL per table, T-PLT-34). This lane names its module `snapshot_dataset.py` (singular, platform area) and its registry `TENANT_SNAPSHOT_INVENTORY` so the two registries never collide; both write `file_object` rows with purpose `SNAPSHOT_DATASET` (PRV-06), which is the shared touchpoint.

## 5. First acceptance slice (this lane's slice 1)

Inventory + JSONL + hash / count round trip with property tests, CPU-only: `snapshot_dataset.py` and `test_snapshot_dataset.py` as in §3, `make lint` and `make typecheck` green, the new tests and `backend/tests/architecture` green. Later slices: the load / reset plan data and its tests (also pure); then, after GATE-RPS / GATE-PLF and a provisioned database, the SNP-1 integration.

## 6. Questions returned to the supervisor (classification is not decided by the lane)

- **Q-1 populations SBX-03 does not name.** `source_*` tables (imported source records feeding `contract_source_link`), `event_submission`, `contract_hold`, `numbering_series`, `registry_version`, `tenant_currency`, `ssp_calculator_*` and `exception_item`: the lane classifies them provisionally (`source_*`, `event_submission`, `contract_hold`, `registry_version`, `tenant_currency` copied as facts / configuration; `numbering_series` copied so numbering continues; `ssp_calculator_*` and `exception_item` excluded as derived) and asks for confirmation.
- **Q-2 approvals "referenced by copied rows".** SBX-03 copies approval requests and decisions referenced by copied rows; slice 2 reads the reference as the `approval_request_id` column of a copied row (20 copied tables carry one) and keeps every step and decision of a kept request; the alternative reading — the request's own `subject_type` / `subject_id` naming a copied row — would also keep requests no copied row points back to (rejected or superseded approvals of copied subjects). Confirm the column reading, or ask for the subject reading as well (both are pure additions).
- **Q-3 period states and locks.** SBX-04 loads every period `open` and replays states and locks "to match the source"; the lane carries `period_state`, `period_state_transition` and `period_lock` as REPLAY_REFERENCE datasets (copied for the replay driver, not loaded as rows). Confirm, or name the replay source otherwise.
- **Q-4 data-retention judgment.** Whether a stored snapshot (`STORED_BACKUP`) inherits the source's retention and legal-hold state, and whether shredding a source file must shred its snapshot copy — an accounting / data-retention judgment for the supervisor (P8 touchpoint).

- **Q-5 credential-like column in a copied table** (returned by lane-f-snp-prep, 2026-09-19 17:3x PDT). `tenant_membership.invitation_token_sha256` travels with the copied membership rows; a production invitation token would then be accepted by the sandbox copy of an `INVITED` membership. Engineering default applied in slice 3: null-on-export for that column, tested; the supervisor may rule otherwise.
- **Q-6 cutoff strictness.** Slice 1 cuts every copied table at `created_at ≤ known_at`; SBX-03 names only `contract_event` (`recorded_at`). Configuration rows created after `known_at` but effective earlier are dropped by the stricter rule. Kept as is in slice 3; confirm or rule.
- **Q-7 the REGENERATED reading of non-secret exclusions** (D-98 candidate 25 leaves no plain "excluded" class). Slice 3 classes `security_event` (C3 security log), `saved_view`, `notification_preference`, `approval_delegation`, `access_review_campaign` / `access_review_item`, `webhook_delivery`, `outbox_message`, `job`, `notification`, `idempotency_record` and the audit chain tables as REGENERATED with the reason "the sandbox produces its own"; tables without `tenant_id` (`currency`, `permission`, `registry_parameter`, `import_template`, `report_definition`, `engine_release`, `app_user`, `tenant`, `identity_provider`, the global identity tables) carry the engineering addition SHARED (read in place; not tenant tables, so outside the ruling's five) except the secret-bearing ones, which are EXCLUDED_SECRET. Confirm the readings or name a sixth class.

## 9. Adoption by lane-f-snp-prep (2026-09-19, supervisor ruling (b))

- **Provenance.** Commits fc81573, aa5dd3d, 98da3be, e050fd7 and f853645 were authored in this worktree by the ENG-C8 writer (lane-eng-c7-loss) under a retracted F-SNP assignment; the supervisor stopped that writer and ruled (b): lane-f-snp-prep adopts the worktree as sole writer, keeps fc81573 as the plan record and extends it, reviews the hand-off modules against its own reading and records the decisions. The hand-off state message named head f853645, clean; verified (`git status` clean, no process of the previous writer). Everything above §9 is the previous writer's text, kept verbatim except the two corrections marked in §2 and §3.
- **Review of the hand-off (read-only, against `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` and the live metadata).** Kept: the inventory shape, the canonical JSONL codec, the cutoff, the manifest, `source_untouched`, the export filters and the SBX-05 comparison — they pass their tests and match the spec. Defects found, fixed in slice 3: (1) the metadata declares no `ForeignKey` objects, so `test_load_order_places_every_parent_first` validated only the hand-written `PARENTS`; against the 04 "FK →" notes `LOAD_ORDER` loaded parents after children — `contract.combination_group_id` (NOT NULL) after `contract`, `combination_group_member.join_event_id` (NOT NULL) after `contract_event`, and the nullable `contract_source_link.contract_event_id`, `fx_rate_set_version.import_upload_id`, `ssp_book_version.import_upload_id`, `registry_version.impact_simulation_file_id`, `role_assignment.approval_request_id`, `role_assignment.sod_exception_id`, `sod_exception.approval_request_id`, `period_state.current_lock_id`; there is a true cycle contract → combination_group → combination_group_member → contract_event → contract; (2) copied rows reference REGENERATED or absent tables (`contract.latest_computation_id`, `combination_group.head_computation_id`, `approval_decision.delegation_id`, `period_state_transition.close_run_id`, `source_record.sync_run_id`, `estimate.portfolio_id`, `contract_event.modification_id`, `contract_event.sync_run_id`, `import_upload.job_id`) with no column-level rule; (3) `tenant_membership.invitation_token_sha256` travels (Q-5); (4) the reset plan named a route that does not exist (`POST /tenant/sandboxes/{id}/reset`; the catalogue has `POST /tenant/reset`); (5) `PENDING_TABLES` held only `tenant_snapshot` while 30 spec tables are absent on main; (6) `modification` is a spec table (T-CON-06, F-CTR), not an event type — my own first report to the supervisor said otherwise and is corrected here.
- **Personal data in copied datasets (P8 touchpoint).** `file_object.original_filename` (PRV-01 C3) and the PRV-05 tokenised JSONB payloads `import_row.raw`, `source_record.payload`, `contract_event.payload`, `manual_adjustment.payload` travel in the snapshot as they are stored; no clear personal data is added by the pipeline.

## 10. Rulings applied (supervisor, D-98 candidate 25) and slice 3

Rulings received 2026-09-19 (engineering-adjacent, not attributed to Ray): **Q-1** every table carries an explicit class — COPIED, REGENERATED, EXCLUDED_SECRET, REPLAY_REFERENCE, PENDING — with a drift guard that fails when a metadata table is unclassified or a PENDING table appears on main; `source_*`, `event_submission`, `contract_hold`, `numbering_series`, `registry_version`, `tenant_currency` and `ssp_calculator_*` are COPIED, `exception_item` REGENERATED, each with a one-line reason. **Q-2** the reference rule is data per approval-bearing table, the filter is pure, export stays with the integration lane. **Q-3** `period_state`, `period_state_transition` and `period_lock` are REPLAY_REFERENCE; ordering contract by transition time then id; a pure replay-plan builder. **Q-4** a `STORED_BACKUP` snapshot inherits the source's retention class and legal-hold state at snapshot time; the manifest records every `file_object` id it contains so a source shred locates every snapshot copy; a legal hold on the source blocks shredding of both — **supervisor ruling; requires human confirmation (Ray / Legal) before production use**; catalogue attributes coordinated with P8. Adopted as this lane's findings: the PENDING set with drift guard; the codec refuses LargeBinary secret columns (`user_mfa_factor.secret_ciphertext`, `webhook_endpoint.secret_ciphertext` never snapshotted — tested exclusion) and requires tz-aware datetimes through `erev_engine.canonical`; INET values normalised with `str()` as `audit/chain.py` does.

Slice 3 (this lane), pure and CPU-only:

| Module | Change | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| `snapshot_dataset.py` | `SnapshotClass` (the five ruled classes + SHARED for tables without `tenant_id`); `RULES: name → (class, SBX-03 category, one-line reason)` for the 130 metadata tables and the 30 PENDING spec tables (planned class and owning lane); `inventory()` fails on an unclassified table, a stale entry or a PENDING table present; `references(metadata)` — every `*_id` column of a copied table resolved to its target by name or by `ALIASES`, or listed in `NON_REFERENCES` (principals, external ids, polymorphic subjects), nullability from the metadata — the metadata declares no foreign keys, so this is the load-order oracle; `LOAD_ORDER` reordered so every NOT NULL reference's target precedes its child; `DEFERRED_REFERENCES` (nullable, target later in the order) restored by a `fixup-deferred-references` step; references to REGENERATED, PENDING or EXCLUDED_SECRET targets are nulled on export (`export_row`) and rebuilt by recompute or left NULL; `SECRET_COLUMNS` nulled on export (`tenant_membership.invitation_token_sha256`, Q-5) with a name-pattern drift guard (`*_ciphertext`, `token`, `secret`, `password`, `recovery_code`, LargeBinary); INET normalisation in the codec; reset plan route `POST /tenant/reset` | `test_snapshot_dataset.py`: class totals pinned, every rule has a reason, pending set pinned, drift guards (unclassified / stale / pending-present), references resolved or declared, NOT NULL parents precede, deferred references nullable, no copied table references an EXCLUDED_SECRET target through a NOT NULL column, secret columns nulled, INET encoded, Hypothesis round trip kept |
| `snapshot_export.py` | `APPROVAL_RULES` as data (column rule per approval-bearing table; subject rule through `approvals.subjects.SUBJECTS[...].table`); `subject_references(requests, copied_ids)`; a request whose subject type has no spec yet is refused (fail closed) | `test_snapshot_export.py`: rules pinned, subject rule keeps a request whose subject row is copied and drops one whose subject is not, union with the column rule |
| `snapshot_replay.py` (new) | Q-3: `ReplayStep`, `replay_plan(states, transitions, locks)` ordered by (`created_at`, `id`) with each lock emitted after its transition; validation against `domain.reference.periods.ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS`, per-period chain contiguity, lock ↔ transition consistency, `previous_lock_id` order; `end_state(plan)` compared with the source `period_state` rows | `test_snapshot_replay.py`: order contract, Hypothesis permutation invariance, invalid pair refused, broken chain refused, end state ties |
| `snapshot_dataset.py` manifest | Q-4: `Retention(files: FileRetention(id, sha256, purpose, retention_until, legal_hold), source_legal_hold)` in the manifest (format version 2); `shred_targets(manifest, file_id)`; `shred_check(manifest, file_id, today)` mirroring `FILE_RETENTION_ACTIVE` and the ruled inheritance | `test_snapshot_dataset.py`: manifest v2 bytes, targets located, shred refused under hold / future retention / inherited hold |

## 11. Rulings D-98 candidate 26, Q-7 confirmation, coordination replies and slice 4

Received 2026-09-19 (supervisor; engineering-adjacent, not attributed to Ray) after ebbbe53 / 1a41066:

- **Q-2 (sharpened).** Both readings: an approval request is exported when a copied row's `approval_request_id` names it OR when its own `subject_type` / `subject_id` names a copied row; union, with every step and decision, rejected and superseded requests included; steps or decisions of an unknown request refused. 1a41066 has both rules and the refusal; slice 4 adds the union function `referenced_approvals()` and the rejected-request test.
- **Q-5.** `tenant_membership.invitation_token_sha256` never travels: an EXCLUDED column, nulled on export, tested; the column class extends to every `*_token_sha256` / secret-derived column found by a metadata scan, with a drift guard. 1a41066 nulls the column and fails the inventory on an undeclared credential-like column; slice 4 renames the data to `EXCLUDED_COLUMNS`, makes the scan explicit (`scan_excluded_columns()`, suffix `_token_sha256` named) and pins its result.
- **Q-6.** The cutoff is per table: fact and event tables by their recorded / applied / decided / created timestamp; configuration and version tables by their effective state as of `known_at` (a version created after `known_at` but effective on or before it IS included; versions effective after `known_at` are not); the per-table rule lives in the inventory data with a drift guard. Replaces the slice-1 "created_at ≤ known_at for every copied table" rule. Slice 4: `CutoffRule` (`timestamp(column)`, `effective(column)`, `parent`, `none`) per dataset; effective columns from the metadata (`effective_from` on the eight IM-P version tables, `valid_from` on `product_bundle_component`, `sod_exception`, `role_assignment`, `effective_date` on `fx_rate`).
- **Finding 1.** Parent graph derived from the 04 "FK →" notes; cycle-aware two-phase load (insert with deferred nullable references, second pass restoring them); recorded as data with a test that every 04 FK edge is honoured or explicitly deferred. 1a41066 derives the graph from the metadata column names (the metadata declares no `ForeignKey`), orders every NOT NULL parent first (`contract.combination_group_id` and `combination_group_member.join_event_id` cannot be inserted NULL, so the order, not a second pass, resolves them) and defers the nullable ones; slice 4 adds the test that reads `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` and checks every "FK →" edge of a copied table against `references()` and the load order.
- **Finding 2.** Column-level null-on-load rules for references to EXCLUDED / REGENERATED / PENDING tables, each with the rebuild step that restores it; `PENDING` gains `sync_run` and `portfolio` (all 29 absent spec tables are in). Slice 4: `REBUILD_STEPS` data per nulled reference (`recompute` for `contract.latest_computation_id`, `combination_group.head_computation_id`, `manual_adjustment.subledger_posting_id`; none — stays NULL, recorded — for `approval_decision.delegation_id`, `period_state_transition.close_run_id`, the PENDING targets `sync_run`, `portfolio`, `modification`, and the operational `job_id` columns), tested against the plan's step names.
- **Finding 4.** Personal-data columns in copied datasets listed for P8 and the Q-4 flag: `file_object.original_filename` (C3, PRV-01); `import_row.raw`, `source_record.payload`, `contract_event.payload`, `manual_adjustment.payload` — P8's catalogue classes these four as **C2 containers** of tokenised (PRV-05) or pseudonymous (PRV-03) references, not C3 (§9 called them "PRV-05 tokenised JSONB payloads"; corrected reading recorded here).
- **Q-7 (confirmed, sixth class).** SHARED = the tables without `tenant_id` (`currency`, `permission`, `registry_parameter`, `import_template`, `report_definition`, `engine_release`, `app_user`, `tenant`): read in place, never copied, never regenerated; the manifest records the identities / versions it depends on for the drift check. The secret-bearing global identity tables are EXCLUDED_SECRET. The REGENERATED reading is confirmed for `security_event`, `saved_view`, `notification_preference`, `approval_delegation`, `access_review_*`, `webhook_delivery`, `outbox_message`, `job`, `notification`, `idempotency_record` and the audit-chain tables, with the addition that the manifest states explicitly that source audit history is NOT carried into the sandbox (the chain starts fresh). Slice 4: `SHARED_KEYS` (which copied columns name which shared identities: `tenant_membership.user_id` → `app_user`; the currency columns → `currency`; `role_permission.permission_code`, `approval_step.required_permission`, `sod_rule.function_*_permissions` → `permission`; `import_upload` / `import_mapping_profile` `template_code` (+ `template_version`) → `import_template`), `shared_dependencies()`, the manifest `shared` block with `engine_release` supplied by the exporter, `verify_shared()`, and `audit_history_carried = false` with its statement. `registry_parameter` identities are tracked through `policy_override.policy_key` (a 04 "FK →" edge on a code column); the parameter keys inside `registry_version.values` are checked by the registry drift tests of the target deployment, not by the manifest (recorded).

Coordination replies:

| From | Reply | Applied |
|---|---|---|
| P8 (lane-p8-privacy-catalogue) | The catalogue attribute is `retention`, a table-level `Retention` literal (`TENANT_LIFETIME`, `AUDIT_RETENTION_YEARS`, `EXPIRES_AT_SWEEP`, `FILE_RETENTION`) at `sprint/l16` ecadc12 (not on main); per-row state stays T-PLT-29 `retention_until` / `legal_hold`. Copied `file_object` rows: `FILE_RETENTION`; `import_row`, `source_record`, `contract_event`, `manual_adjustment`: `AUDIT_RETENTION_YEARS` (PROPOSED); `tenant_snapshot`: `TENANT_LIFETIME` (PROPOSED). `original_filename` is the only C3 column among the copies (erasure NO_PATH today; D-97 §C (39) proposal); the exclusions match the catalogue's C3 / credential set; `invitation_token_sha256` C3 with erasure CLEARED_ON_USE, so nulling it is consistent. Caveats: unnamed classes are PROPOSED until counsel confirms; the catalogue is not on main. | Slice 4: `RETENTION_ATTRIBUTE = "retention"`, `RETENTION_FAMILIES` (the literals above as strings, marked PROPOSED) in the manifest retention block; no import of the P8 module until it merges. |
| F-RPS / E1 (lane-eng-b1) | Registries stay disjoint (`domain/reports/snapshots.py::SNAPSHOT_DATASETS`, S15-R-19 manifest lines) and the file purpose `SNAPSHOT_DATASET` is unchanged; `lock_snapshot` REGENERATED matches its Q-5 ruling; recorded in `F-RPS-ENG-E1-prep.md` §8 at 635c509. | Nothing further. |

Assumptions recorded for the supervisor (Q-8, engineering) — **all three confirmed by the supervisor (D-98 candidate 30, 2026-09-19)**; the entity-local refinement of DATE effectivity belongs to the integration lane, recorded as such:

- **Q-8a master data without effectivity.** Non-versioned configuration rows (`legal_entity`, `book`, `gl_account`, `dimension_*`, `pob_template`, `product`, `role`, `tenant_membership`, `numbering_series`, …) carry no row history (IM-M, DB-02 touch trigger). Slice 4 cuts them by `created_at ≤ known_at` (the row existed at `known_at`) and copies their **current** state; a later update is not undone. Child rows of a version (`rule`, `rule_test_case`, `account_mapping_rule`, `ssp_entry`, `ssp_range`, `role_permission`) follow their parent version (`parent` rule).
- **Q-8b effective dates.** `effective_from` columns are timestamps and compare with `known_at` directly; `valid_from` / `effective_date` DATE columns compare with the UTC calendar date of `known_at` (business dates are entity-local, API-C-07; a per-entity refinement is the integration lane's call). A version whose effective column is NULL (DRAFT, TESTED) is kept when `created_at ≤ known_at`.
- **Q-8c lost lineage.** `approval_decision.delegation_id` stays NULL in a sandbox (delegations are REGENERATED); the decision keeps `approver_id` and `on_behalf_of_id`, so the on-behalf lineage remains readable from the copied row, and the source audit chain (not carried) keeps the full record.

Slice 4 (this lane), pure and CPU-only — module changes: `snapshot_dataset.py` (`CutoffRule` / `CUTOFF_RULES` with drift guard; `EXCLUDED_COLUMNS` + `scan_excluded_columns()`; `REBUILD_STEPS`; `SHARED_KEYS` / `shared_dependencies()` / `verify_shared()`; manifest `shared` block, `audit_history_carried`, `RETENTION_ATTRIBUTE` / `RETENTION_FAMILIES`), `snapshot_export.py` (`referenced_approvals()` union), tests (per-table cutoff incl. the "created after, effective before" case and the "effective after" exclusion; scan pinned; rebuild steps complete; 04 FK edges honoured or deferred; shared block and audit statement; union and rejected-request inclusion).

### 11.1 Slice 4 outcome

Committed as 56aea27 (docs first in 3f44319). ## 12. Codex independent review of 1a41066 (PRODUCTION-FSNP-SLICE3-INDEPENDENT-1a41066.md; raw 40/53) and the fixes (b00a7df)

Relayed by the supervisor 2026-09-19 (document SHA-256 96fd5012…, manifest 1c119eba…; native 33/36, attachments 1/3, replay 6/14). Ten failed assertions = four findings, fixed on top of 56aea27, fail-first (the nine new tests failed on 56aea27 before the fix: `.run/F-SNP/fail-first-R1-R4.txt`), lint-gated, in b00a7df:

| Finding | Defect (at 1a41066 / 56aea27) | Ruling and fix |
|---|---|---|
| **F-SNP-R1** load steps disagree with deferral | `load_plan` executed the configuration family before the fact family while deferral was derived from the global `LOAD_ORDER`, so six nullable references from configuration versions to facts that load earlier in `LOAD_ORDER` (`rule_set_version` / `account_mapping_version` / `registry_version` `.impact_simulation_file_id` → `file_object`; `fx_rate_set_version.import_upload_id`, `ssp_book_version.import_upload_id` → `import_upload`; `ssp_book_version.ssp_calculator_run_id` → `ssp_calculator_run`) were neither deferred nor honoured by the executed order | ONE sequence: `load_plan` emits one `load:<table>` step per COPIED dataset in exactly `LOAD_ORDER`; the test checks the executed order equals `LOAD_ORDER` and every nullable reference to a later step is deferred with its value preserved by `export_row`; the six references are honoured by the order (their parents load first) |
| **F-SNP-R2** approval population + Iterable contract | a referenced request id absent from the population returned empty (the missing-file check refuses); `requests` was iterated twice, so an iterator input falsely raised "unknown request" | inputs materialised once; a referenced request missing from the population is refused (same shape as `filter_files`); complete-list, unrelated-exclusion and unknown-step controls kept |
| **F-SNP-R3** attachments lack copied-subject filtering | `file_attachment.subject_id` is a `NON_REFERENCE`, so a reconciliation (REGENERATED) or modification (PENDING) subject kept its attachment and file | `ATTACHMENT_SUBJECTS` (the twelve T-PLT-30 literals → tables, pinned against the 04 CHECK by a test that reads the doc) and `filter_attachments()`: an attachment travels only with a copied subject row; a REGENERATED subject's attachment is excluded; a PENDING or unsupported subject type fails closed; files are referenced only through kept attachments (the copied-contract positive stays) |
| **F-SNP-R4** replay identity tuple not joined | transitions were checked for state-id existence only, locks matched on period only, `verify_end_state` compared state and lock id only | `replay_plan` requires the (entity, book, period) tuple of every transition to equal its period state's and of every lock to equal its transition's; `EndState` carries the identity and `verify_end_state` reports a differing source row; the five mutation controls (transition entity / book / period; lock entity / book) are tests |

Codex's three further replay failures are malformed-input hardening that migration 0029 already constrains at the database (recorded as such, not as defects). Passing controls Codex confirms at 1a41066, recorded: all 130 tables classified once; 29 pending; credential / pending-table refusal; invitation hash nulling; binary refusal; all 12 declared unsafe references nulled; nullable-cycle fixup; four cutoff boundaries and the naive-cutoff refusal; stable numeric codec; `known_at` and group / book population checks. Unproved and open (integration after GATE-RPS / GATE-PLF on provisioned databases): the migration, the job, the routes, the file writer, the real loader and replay; the retention policy needs human confirmation (Ray / Legal).

Observation (R3-adjacent, no behaviour change, for the integration lane): `judgement_record.subject_type` / `subject_id` and `rule_test_case.subject_type` / `subject_id` are polymorphic too (04 checks name `contract`, `obligation`, `combination_group`, `modification`, `estimate_version`, `product`, `registry_version`, `migration_batch`, and the four version tables); those rows are COPIED facts / configuration in their own right (SBX-03 names judgement records), so they travel, and a subject that is PENDING (`modification`, `migration_batch`) dangles until its table lands — recorded here rather than filtered, because SBX-03 copies judgement records unconditionally.

One requirement from the retest, done before the candidate-34 commit: the original direct export chain scored 1/3 because nothing called the new selector — `select_export()` (e15e772) now selects attachments and their files only through `filter_polymorphic` / `filter_attachments`, with a chain test that fails on the direct chain (the reconciliation attachment's file is referenced there) and passes on the composed one.

**Ruling D-98 candidate 34** (`judgement_record`, `rule_test_case` polymorphic subjects; ac4965a + d98462b): the same fail-closed treatment as attachments — a PENDING or unsupported subject type refuses the export until the table lands (never a dangling reference); a REGENERATED subject keeps the row with `subject_id` nulled on export and a `REBUILD_STEPS` entry "stays NULL"; a copied subject is kept as is. **Process slip recorded.** ac4965a was committed while **four** tests failed (junit: tests=138, failures=4; the fourth, omitted from the first record text, is `test_regenerated_polymorphic_subjects_are_nulled_and_recorded` in its original `exception_item`-as-judgement form, later separated into the widened-map characterisation): the lane's gate script grepped a `head`-truncated pytest output, so the summary line with " failed" was cut off and the commit proceeded. Not amended (never amend); corrected by d98462b; the script now reads the final `N passed` / `N failed` summary line on its own and refuses on any failure. `pytest-17/` remains the original failed run, kept as producer-designated evidence (not an authenticated execution of ac4965a).

Code and interface dependencies are separated from gate-run dependencies: GATE-RPS and GATE-PLF are gate **runs**, so they do not block writing the SNP-1 source; DB execution, schema application and acceptance stay behind the Ray-side `erev_rv_l23_*` boundary and are stated per item. One writer per path: this lane's paths are the sandbox / snapshot modules it owns (`domain/platform/snapshot_*.py`), the new job / route / writer files it creates, the T-PLT-34 revision file and its tests; anything in another lane's files (P2 `jobs/context` or the file store, F-LMG migration modules) is a request to that lane, named by symbol and owner.

Slices, each docs-first and lint-gated on `sprint/l23-fsnp`, validated without a database (lint, typecheck, unit tests with mocked units of work / file store, architecture and layer tests, the OpenAPI check):

| Slice | Content | DB boundary |
|---|---|---|
| I-1 | T-PLT-34 `tenant_snapshot` Alembic revision with a **lane-local provisional filename / id and a placeholder `down_revision`** (the supervisor assigns the final number and parent at merge — provisional sequence P2 0055 → C1b 0056 → F-ADM 0057 / 0058 → F-LMG 0059 → F-SNP next; the lane does not renumber); the `Table` in `db/tables/platform.py`; DB-03 transitions for `tenant_snapshot`; DB-15 `tg_tenant_snapshot__target` | `alembic upgrade`, `test_upgrade_downgrade_upgrade`, the isolation suite and `test_db_15_snapshot_target_must_be_sandbox` are written, **not run** (databases not provisioned) |
| I-2 | `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` job handler: worker registration, idempotency, refusal paths, `select_export` with an explicit `known_at`; removed from `PENDING_JOB_HANDLERS` | handler unit-tested with a mocked unit of work; the job run itself not executed |
| I-3 | the `put_file` JSONL writer for the export: streaming, one file per table, the manifest with digests (`SNAPSHOT_DATASET`, PRV-06) | writer unit-tested against an in-memory / temporary-directory file store; no `file_object` row written |

### 13.1 Slice I-1 — T-PLT-34 `tenant_snapshot` (docs first)

- **Revision (provisional).** File `backend/erev_api/db/migrations/versions/0099_snp_1_tenant_snapshot.py`, `revision = "0099"`, `down_revision = "0054"` — both provisional: 0099 keeps the lane's revision outside the sequence the supervisor assigns at merge (P2 0055 → C1b 0056 → F-ADM 0057 / 0058 → F-LMG 0059 → F-SNP next) and matches the `NNNN_slug.py` pattern `code_head` reads, so `make lint`'s single-head check holds on this branch; `down_revision = "0054"` is the placeholder parent (the merged head's last revision) that the supervisor reparents at merge. The lane does not renumber. Content: `create_tenant_table("tenant_snapshot", …, standard_sets=["SC-C"])` with the T-PLT-34 columns (`known_at`, `purpose` with `ck_tenant_snapshot__purpose`, `status erev.run_status` default `'QUEUED'`, `target_tenant_id`, `manifest_file_id`, `manifest_sha256`, `row_counts`, `job_id`, `started_at`, `finished_at`), `ix_tenant_snapshot__status`; foreign keys `target_tenant_id → tenant` (global), `manifest_file_id → file_object`, `job_id → job`; class IM-S with the 04 UPDATE list; RLS-T; DB-03 `tg_tenant_snapshot__transition` from `transition_trigger_sql("tenant_snapshot")` (E-67 pairs; `manifest_file_id`, `manifest_sha256`, `started_at`, `finished_at` set once); DB-15 `tg_tenant_snapshot__target` (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE): a non-NULL `target_tenant_id` must name a tenant of kind `sandbox` visible to the invoker — trigger functions are SECURITY INVOKER, so a target hidden by RLS-TN is refused too (fail closed); the SNP-2 load sets the target under provisioning scope, where the tenant row is visible. Downgrade: `drop_tenant_table` (drops the table and its `tg_tenant_snapshot__*` functions).
- **Table.** `erev_api.db.tables.platform.tenant_snapshot` with exactly the 04 columns (DG-ARC-09 compares names); exported from `erev_api.db.tables`.
- **Transitions.** `TRANSITIONS["tenant_snapshot"]`: status pairs `_RUN_STATUS_PAIRS`; updatable `target_tenant_id`, `manifest_file_id`, `manifest_sha256`, `row_counts`, `started_at`, `finished_at`; set-once `manifest_file_id`, `manifest_sha256`, `started_at`, `finished_at`; the pinned set in `tests/unit/test_transitions.py` gains the table.
- **Row builder.** `tests/support/rows.py::tenant_snapshot_values` and the `ROW_BUILDERS` entry, so the isolation suite (DG-TST-20) covers the table.
- **Inventory.** `tenant_snapshot` leaves `PENDING` for `RULES` (REGENERATED, SNAPSHOT_BOOKKEEPING) — the drift guard's intended path when a pending table lands; class totals become REGENERATED 41 / PENDING 28.
- **Tests.** CPU: `tests/unit/test_tenant_snapshot_schema.py` (revision constants pinned as provisional; `TRANSITION_BODY` equals the registry rendering; the DB-15 body names `EREV-SBX-001` and `sandbox`; the row builder covers every NOT NULL column; DG-ARC-09 and the pinned transitions set pass). DB (written, **not run** — databases not provisioned): `tests/pg/test_db_invariants.py::test_db_15_snapshot_target_must_be_sandbox` (a production target raises `EREV-SBX-001`; a sandbox target under provisioning scope succeeds); `test_upgrade_downgrade_upgrade` and the isolation suite cover the revision and the table by construction.
- **DB boundary.** `alembic upgrade`, the round trip, the isolation suite and the DB-15 test execute only on the Ray-side `erev_rv_l23_*` databases; nothing here runs them.

### 13.2 Slice I-2 — the `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` job handler (docs first)

- **Module.** `backend/erev_api/domain/platform/snapshot_job.py` (new; this lane's path): `@task(JobKind.TENANT_SNAPSHOT, retry=RetryPolicy(max_attempts=1), on_failure=snapshot_failed)` per the 05 §5.6 row (retry none; queue `maintenance` already in `JOB_QUEUE`). Params `{tenant_snapshot_id, known_at (RFC 3339 UTC), purpose}`; `known_at` is explicit and passed to `select_export` / `cutoff` — never defaulted inside the handler.
- **Refusal paths** (each a `Problem`, each tested): missing or malformed params → `validation-failed`; `known_at` later than `uow.now` → `validation-failed` (`KNOWN_AT_FUTURE`); a `purpose` outside T-PLT-34 → `validation-failed`; the T-PLT-34 row not found → `not-found`; a row already `SUCCEEDED` → idempotent return of its manifest (no second export); `FAILED` → `invalid-transition`; a sandbox source tenant → `sandbox-restricted` (SBX-02: snapshots copy a production tenant). `QUEUED → RUNNING` sets `started_at` and audits `tenant.snapshot_started`; the copy lock `tenant-copy:<source tenant>` (05 §5.6) is taken as a transaction advisory lock by the DB repository; the source is read in repeatable-read batches per dataset (SBX-04) with a job heartbeat per dataset.
- **Composition.** `export_snapshot(repo, reader, writer, *, known_at, purpose, job_id, now)` is the pure orchestration over three protocols — `SnapshotRepository` (load / transition / audit the T-PLT-34 row), `SnapshotReader` (rows per dataset as of `known_at`, row counts for SBX-11), `SnapshotWriter` (one file per dataset + the manifest; the `put_file` implementation is slice I-3) — so the unit tests drive it with fakes and the DB adapters stay thin: per dataset in `LOAD_ORDER`, `cutoff` at `known_at`, then `select_export` over the cut rows (attachments, approvals, files) with the copied-id populations from the cut rows, `encode_rows` → writer, `retention_of` + `shared_dependencies` → `Manifest` (format 2), `source_untouched(before, after)` refuses a changed source, then `RUNNING → SUCCEEDED` with `manifest_file_id`, `manifest_sha256`, `row_counts`, `finished_at` and the audit `tenant.snapshot_created` (SBX-06). `snapshot_failed` (the failure hook) moves `QUEUED` / `RUNNING` → `FAILED` with the job's problem and audits `tenant.snapshot_failed`.
- **Registration.** The handler registers through `jobs.registry.task` when its module is imported; the worker imports `erev_api.worker.HANDLER_MODULES` (P2's file) — **cross-owner dependency:** symbol `erev_api.worker.HANDLER_MODULES`, owner P2 (lane-p2-hosted-providers), one line `"erev_api.domain.platform.snapshot_job"`, requested by message; until it lands `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` stays in `PENDING_JOB_HANDLERS` (the registries test imports the worker) and the route module does not import the handler module. The `PENDING_JOB_HANDLERS` removal (this lane's test file) follows P2's line.
- **Tests.** CPU: `tests/unit/test_snapshot_job.py` — the orchestration with fake repository / reader / writer (happy path: files per copied dataset, manifest digests, row counts, `SUCCEEDED`; each refusal path; idempotent `SUCCEEDED` return; source-changed refusal; failure hook transitions). DB (written, not run): `tests/domain/platform/test_snapshots.py::test_snapshot_copies_facts_not_derived`, `::test_known_at_cutoff`, `::test_manifest_hashes_and_counts`, `::test_source_unchanged_and_audited` (BUILD_SPEC SNP-1 names) through `run_inline`.
- **DB boundary.** The handler executes only on the worker against a provisioned database; here it is imported, type-checked and unit-tested with fakes.

### 13.1.1 Rulings at the I-1 close

- **Provisional revision.** First ruling (after report 12): a non-numeric id `snp1_tenant_snapshot` in a file outside the numbered sequence. This lane pointed out that `migration_ops._revision_graph` reads only `NNNN_slug.py` files, so `code_head()` (used by `GET /readyz`, the release manifest and `erev doctor`) would keep reporting `0054` while `alembic heads` reported the provisional id, and a provisioned database with the revision applied would read as out of step. **Ruling change (after report 13): do NOT rename** — keep `0099_snp_1_tenant_snapshot.py` with `revision = "0099"` and `down_revision = "0054"`; 0099 is far outside the 0055–0061 sequence and cannot collide (F-LMG's T-MIG draft takes 0098 under the same convention); the file header and this record state: PROVISIONAL — number and parent assigned by the supervisor at merge; the lane never renumbers. c04f5d2 already satisfies this; no further source change.
- **Lock.** Implementing the 05 §5.6 `lock = 'tenant-copy:<source tenant>'` inside the snapshot repository as a transaction advisory lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(<string>, 0))`) is accepted as the implementation of the §5.6 lock; no registry-level named lock is requested from P2.
- **Registration dependency.** Accepted as P2 answered: the one `HANDLER_MODULES` line (`"erev_api.domain.platform.snapshot_job"`) lands as P2's post-merge one-line slice with DG-ARC-08 as the check (`worker.py` imports every entry at import time, so the line cannot precede the module on main); until then `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` stays in `PENDING_JOB_HANDLERS` and the route module never imports the handler module; the handler and its fakes-driven tests are written regardless (I-2).
- **Cadence rule** (dispatch-common amendment 20:15 PDT, Codex 0314): one bounded slice per dispatch — the final report for the slice, then the turn ends; the supervisor continues with the next queued prompt. I-1 closes with this record; I-2 (§13.2) starts on the supervisor's word.

### 13.2.1 Slice I-2 outcome

Committed as 2c87ef9b. Everything of §13.2 landed. Details worth the reader's attention: (1) **registration discipline** — importing the handler module registers the kind, and `jobs.registry.task` refuses a second registration, so both test modules import the handler lazily inside a fixture, re-register by hand when the module is already loaded, and deregister at teardown; the DG-ARC-08 registries test (which imports the worker, not this module) runs first by collection rank and never sees `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` registered while it is pending; a unit test pins that `api/v1/tenant.py` does not name the handler module. (2) **The advisory copy lock** is `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('tenant-copy:<tenant>', 0))` inside `DbSnapshotRepository.take_copy_lock`, per the ruling. (3) **`writer_for(uow)` refuses** with `invalid-transition` "slice I-3" until the `put_file` writer lands, so the handler cannot store files yet even if registered. (4) **Reading pattern:** `DbSnapshotReader` reads each dataset in its own read-only tenant transaction and the counts before and after bracket the read; a count that changed refuses the export with `precondition-failed` and the failure hook ends the row `FAILED`. (5) **Defect fixed on the way:** `select_export` now sets the `file_object` dataset to the kept files (Codex's R3/R5 checks read `selection.files`; a table-by-table encoder read `selection.rows`). (6) The written domain tests are scaffolds against the provisioned database: `test_known_at_cutoff` carries a TODO to book a contract and append a later event through the command factories once the database exists.

### 13.2.2 Codex integration review at 2c87ef9b — F-SNP-I2-R1 and the fix slice (docs first)

Relayed by the supervisor: PRODUCTION-F-SNP-INTEGRATION-REVIEW-2c87ef9.md (SHA-256 c5b3c490…, manifest 383d2d7f…, 20 artifacts, 1,695 frozen files); the R5 program and oracles stay 39/39; the new orchestration composition scores 7/10 with three failed assertions for one defect, over supplied rows and inert repository / reader / byte-capture writer protocols (not DB-admitted rows, `put_file` or restore).

**F-SNP-I2-R1 — descendants survive an excluded parent (OPEN → this slice).** `known_at` 30 June: a 29 June draft SSP version 200 with entry 300 / range 400 passes 5/5; adding a 1 July draft version 201 with entry 301 / range 401, the per-table cutoff excludes version 201 (draft `effective_from` NULL, creation-time fallback) but independently keeps entry 301 and range 401 (PARENT rule: every row follows its parent — implemented as "keep every row", not as "follow the parent"), then reports `SUCCEEDED` with valid digests and counts despite the missing version reference. **Ruling D-98 62:** declared PARENT dependencies are applied transitively after each table's own `known_at` cutoff and before selection / encoding; descendants of an excluded parent are excluded; a broken required reference is a refusal before success — never silently nulled, never `SUCCEEDED`; the positive chain stays; every declared PARENT family is covered, not just SSP.

Fix (this slice, fail-first with Codex's exact composition plus one case per other PARENT family):
- `snapshot_dataset.apply_parent_cutoff(inventory, source, cut) -> ParentCutoff(kept, excluded_descendants, broken)`: for every reference of a copied dataset whose target is a copied or replay dataset, a non-NULL value must name a row kept by the target's cutoff; a value naming a row present in the source but cut → the row is an excluded descendant (any nullability); a value naming no source row at all → a broken reference finding (required or not — the source's own foreign keys make it impossible in a real database, so it signals an incomplete read); processed to a fixed point across `LOAD_ORDER` so exclusions cascade through later parents and self-references.
- `export_snapshot` applies it after the per-table cutoff and before `select_export`; a non-empty `broken` refuses with `validation-failed` ("broken required reference") — the job fails and `snapshot_failed` ends the row `FAILED`; the copied-id populations, the encoded files and the counts come from the kept rows; the audit detail records `excluded_descendants` per table.
- **REPEATABLE READ explicitly** (Codex: no source assurance; server defaults not queried): `db.session.tenant_session` gains `isolation_level: Literal["REPEATABLE READ"] | None = None`, executed as the transaction's first statement (`SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ` before the `set_config` SELECT, which PostgreSQL requires); `DbSnapshotReader` reads every dataset and the counts under it; a unit test pins the statement order through a recording connection. Kernel file `db/session.py`: an additive keyword, no behaviour change for existing callers (recorded; not another lane's path).
- **Engine release wiring:** the handler reads `controls.release.current_release()` and passes `"<engine_version>+<build_sha>"` (T-PLT-38 fields) to the reader; `None` only when no release is stamped (tests, `run_inline`).
- **`test_known_at_cutoff` oracle:** the written domain test books a contract before `known_at`, advances the clock past it, appends a later event through `support.factories.appended`, runs the job and asserts the `contract_event` dataset count equals the count of events with `recorded_at ≤ known_at` and is one less than the table's total — still written, **not run**.
- Out of scope, unchanged: the streaming `put_file` writer (`WRITER_PENDING`, I-3), worker registration / PENDING removal, routes / OpenAPI, the retention configuration with a refusing default, actual load / replay.

### 13.2.3 Slice I-2.1 outcome — the F-SNP-I2-R1 fix (head 9e001b81)

Fail-first on 2c87ef9b (`.run/F-SNP/fail-first-I2-R1.txt`): 10 failed, 45 deselected — Codex's composition (version 201 / entry 301 / range 401), one case per declared PARENT family (role → role_permission, rule_set_version → rule and → rule_test_case, account_mapping_version → account_mapping_rule, approval_request → approval_step, ssp_calculator_run → ssp_calculator_result), the broken-reference refusal, the transaction statement order with the release wiring, and the pure `apply_parent_cutoff` oracle. After the fix: the six lane unit files + `test_transitions.py` + `tests/architecture` 172 passed (`pytest-31/`); `make typecheck` clean (`typecheck-25.log`); `make lint` clean (`lint-23.log`).

What landed, against the plan above:
- `ParentCutoff(kept, excluded_descendants, nulled_as_of, broken)` and `apply_parent_cutoff(inventory, source, cut)`: fixed point over `LOAD_ORDER`; a reference to a kept row passes; a reference to a row in the source but cut drops the row when the table follows its parent (`PARENT` cutoff) **or** the reference is required; a **nullable non-parent** link to a cut row is cleared and counted in `nulled_as_of` (interpretation, not in the plan — see the open point); a reference naming no source row is a broken finding `"<table>.<column>: <id> names <value>, absent from the source"`.
- A judgement record's or rule test case's polymorphic subject (`POLYMORPHIC_SUBJECT_COLUMNS`) naming a COPIED table is a parent in the same sense (not in the plan): `rule_test_case` has no `rule_set_version_id` column — its declared PARENT family runs through `subject_type` / `subject_id` — so "every PARENT family" needed it. REGENERATED and PENDING subjects stay with the candidate-34 filter (nulled / refused). Attachments are unchanged (F-SNP-R3 filter: excluded when the subject is not copied).
- `export_snapshot` keeps the pre-cutoff rows, applies the parent cutoff, refuses a non-empty `broken` with `validation-failed` (`BROKEN_REFERENCE`, one `ProblemError` per finding, `rule_id` SNP-1) before any write, and builds populations / files / counts from the kept rows; the `tenant.snapshot_created` detail carries `excluded_descendants` and `nulled_as_of` per table.
- `db/session.py`: `tenant_session(..., isolation_level: Literal["REPEATABLE READ"] | None = None)`; the transaction setup is the public `transaction_setup(connection, ctx, *, read_only, isolation_level, ...)` emitting `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ` first, then the `set_config` context query, then `SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY`. Additive; existing callers unchanged. `DbSnapshotReader.ISOLATION_LEVEL = "REPEATABLE READ"` for every per-dataset read and the counts.
- `engine_release_of(release) -> "<engine_version>+<build_sha>" | None`; the handler passes `engine_release_of(current_release())` to the reader, so the manifest's `shared.engine_release` is the stamped T-PLT-38 release (None only when none is stamped: tests, `run_inline`).
- `test_known_at_cutoff` oracle (written, **not run**): two events on one contract via `insert_contract_rows(head_stream_version=2)` and `contract_event_values(stream_version=1|2)`, `known_at` read as `SELECT now()` in its own transaction between them — the database clock, not the frozen application clock, because DB-08 stamps `recorded_at` with the server default `now()` (verified on the table: `server_default now()`, builder does not set it), so the plan's "advance the frozen clock" would not move `recorded_at`; asserts `(total, recorded_at ≤ known_at) == (2, 1)` (pins the precondition; a timestamp collision fails loudly) and `row_counts["contract_event"] == 1`.
- Family test data: approvals are copied only with a copied subject (Q-2 both readings), so the approval_request rows anchor on a copied `rule_set_version` (`RULE_SET_VERSION`); the first version of the test used a subject absent from the world and dropped both requests for the right reason.

**Open point for the supervisor (interpretation of D-98 62).** The ruling says descendants of an excluded parent are excluded and a broken *required* reference refuses. A nullable link that is not a parent dependency (e.g. `contract.superseded_by_id`, `judgement_record.contract_id`, `approval_step.required_role_id`) naming a row cut by `known_at` is neither: the row existed as of `known_at`, the link did not. Implemented: the column is cleared and counted (`nulled_as_of` in the result and the audit detail — not silent). Alternative: drop the row as well (one condition in `apply_parent_cutoff`; the docs-first plan said "any nullability"). Either reading passes the ruled tests; the choice changes which rows a sandbox holds, so it is recorded here rather than decided by the lane.

DB boundary this slice: a first full-suite invocation (`pytest-32/`) lost its `--ignore` flags to zsh word splitting and collected the seven DB-fixture unit files: 1951 passed, 1 xfailed, **10 errors**, every error `psycopg.OperationalError: … database "erev_rv_l23_test" does not exist` on 127.0.0.1:5432 — the lane database is not provisioned, nothing was created, the dev database was not addressed. The protocol run with the exclusions is the suite row below.

Isolation caveat (supervisor, on the Codex retest): each dataset read and each count sweep runs in its **own** read-only REPEATABLE READ transaction. The pinned statement order is inert evidence of what the reader asks for; it is not a proof of the server's isolation, and it is not one snapshot across the run — consistency across datasets rests on the SBX-11 count bracket (`precondition-failed` when the counts move), not on a single transaction.

### 13.2.4 Slice I-2.2 — F-SNP-I2-R2 (ruling D-98 71) and the missing-stamp refusal (docs first)

Dispatched by the supervisor after the Codex retest at 9e001b81 (§13.2.3). Two changes, fail-first, one commit; the §13.2.2 plan line "a value naming a row present in the source but cut → the row is an excluded descendant (any nullability)" is the governing wording and stands; the §13.2.3 `nulled_as_of` interpretation is withdrawn.

- **D-98 71 — exclude, never null.** `apply_parent_cutoff`: a non-NULL reference (nullable or not, parent-following table or not) naming a row present in the source but cut by `known_at` excludes the row; no column is ever cleared. `ParentCutoff(kept, excluded_descendants, excluded_ids, broken)`: `excluded_descendants` keeps the per-table counts, `excluded_ids` adds the excluded row ids per table in row order; `nulled_as_of` is removed. The `tenant.snapshot_created` audit detail carries `excluded_descendants` (counts) and `excluded_descendant_ids` (ids as strings), no `nulled_as_of`. Manifest bytes and counts stay derived from what was written. Fail-first with Codex's exact observation: calculator run 500 created 29 June, `draft_ssp_book_version_id` → the 1 July version 201, `known_at` 30 June — expected `ssp_calculator_run` row count 0, run ids written `[]`, the run id in the audit detail; plus the pure oracle. As-of reconstruction of a later-set optional reference: post-rc question (needs row history), not implemented.
- **Missing engine-release stamp refuses before any write.** `Shared` refuses `engine_release` None or empty at construction (`ValueError`); `shared_dependencies` requires the release (no `None` default); `verify_shared` reports a shared block without a release; `export_snapshot` refuses with `precondition-failed` (`RELEASE_UNSTAMPED`) when the reader has no release — after the `RUNNING` transition and before any dataset read or write, so the row ends `FAILED` through `snapshot_failed` with nothing written. The handler keeps passing `engine_release_of(current_release())`; worker startup stamps the release (05 REL-03/05); `engine_release_of(None)` stays `None` (the exporter, not the formatter, refuses). Fakes supply a release (already `"1.0.0"`); the written domain tests stamp through the test-side path `support.factories.stamp_test_release()` in the `job` fixture (the autouse conftest fixture forgets it after each test) — still written, **not run**.
- Out of scope, unchanged: the streaming `put_file` writer (`WRITER_PENDING`), worker registration, routes / OpenAPI, retention configuration, load / replay, DB acceptance.

### 13.2.6 Slice I-3 — the streaming `put_file` writer (docs first)

Dispatched by the supervisor after I-2.2. Replaces the `WRITER_PENDING` stop with the real writer; mock storage adapters and inert protocols only (no live storage, no third-party calls); DB-bound tests written, **not run**.

- **Writer protocol becomes streaming.** `SnapshotWriter.write(*, name, stream: BinaryIO, media_type) -> Mapping` returning the `file_object` row (`id`, `sha256`, `size_bytes`). The exporter hands each dataset as `snapshot_dataset.encode_stream(dataset, rows)` — a `JsonlStream` (`io.RawIOBase`): the exported rows are sorted once by `order_by` (the reader's rows are already in memory), then canonical JSONL lines are produced only as the store reads them, hashed and counted on the way; no whole-dataset byte buffer exists on the exporter's side. `encode_rows` is re-expressed over the same stream (one code path). The manifest entry takes the stream's digest and row count after exhaustion. **Contract change for inert harnesses:** a byte-capture writer reads `stream.read()` instead of receiving `content: bytes` (Codex's programs adapt at retest; recorded here, not a 04 / 05 row).
- **Byte-for-byte equality.** Two checks: the exporter refuses when the row's `sha256` (what the store hashed while spooling) differs from the stream's digest (what was hashed on the way out); `PutFileWriter` reads the stored file back through `files.store.open_file` and refuses when the read-back digest differs from the row's. Both refusals: `precondition-failed` (`STORED_MISMATCH`), before the manifest, never `SUCCEEDED`.
- **Empty datasets are not stored.** T-PLT-29 stores at least one byte (`put_file` refuses a zero-byte non-upload), so a dataset with no rows gets a manifest entry `(name, sha256 of the empty content, 0)` and no file; `verify_manifest` accepts a missing file for exactly such an entry; the load step for that table loads nothing. Interpretation recorded for the supervisor — the alternative is a 04 change allowing zero-byte `file_object` rows, which is not the lane's to make.
- **`PutFileWriter(uow)`** (in `snapshot_job`): `put_file(uow, purpose=FilePurpose.SNAPSHOT_DATASET, stream=…, original_filename=name, media_type=…)` — E-68 purpose, encrypted at rest (PRV-06 `ENCRYPTED_PURPOSES`), content-addressed per `(tenant, sha256, purpose)` so identical bytes dedupe to one row; then the read-back check. The store's own encryption path holds the plaintext of one file at a time (`spool.read()` / `open_file` — kernel behaviour, not this lane's). Writing as the source tenant's system principal: the dataset files and the manifest are `file_object` rows of the source tenant (T-PLT-34 `manifest_file_id → file_object`).
- **Write error → refusal before success, no partial manifest, written datasets named.** Any exception from `writer.write` (store error, mismatch) is wrapped as `Problem("precondition-failed", STORAGE_FAILED, errors=[ProblemError(field="written", rule_id="SNP-1-WRITTEN", message=<names in write order>), ProblemError(field=<dataset>, rule_id="SNP-1-WRITE", message=<error class>)])` — the error class only, never its message (DG-LOG-03) — raised before the manifest is composed; `fail_snapshot` copies the problem's `errors`, `written` and `failed` into the `tenant.snapshot_failed` detail. Transitions stay `QUEUED → RUNNING → SUCCEEDED | FAILED`. The datasets already stored are named, not deleted (content-addressed rows; the next successful snapshot reuses identical bytes).
- **Tests.** Unit, fakes: stream per dataset (the writer receives a `JsonlStream`), hash equality entry ↔ captured bytes ↔ row, empty datasets unstored with the empty digest, store-hash mismatch refusal, write-error refusal naming the written datasets and the failure audit carrying them, `writer_for` returning `PutFileWriter`, `PutFileWriter` over monkeypatched `put_file` / `open_file` (agreeing and disagreeing read-back). Pure: `encode_stream` laziness (row count advances as lines are read) and digest equality with `encode_rows`; `verify_manifest` with an unstored empty entry. DB-bound (`tests/domain/platform/test_snapshots.py`, written NOT RUN): the manifest test skips file look-up for empty entries; a `FailingStore` adapter (wraps `LocalFileStore`, raises on the third `put`) ends the job and the row `FAILED` with the written datasets named in the failure audit and no `manifest_file_id`.
- Unchanged: worker registration (P2 post-merge line; `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` stays in `PENDING_JOB_HANDLERS`), routes / OpenAPI (I-4), retention configuration (I-5), load / replay.

### 13.2.7 Slice I-3 outcome (head fd9254ca)

Fail-first on 1cd0f7be (`.run/F-SNP/fail-first-I3.txt`): 6 failed, 59 deselected — the fakes still received `content: bytes` (three `TypeError`s), no `put_file` on the module, no `encode_stream`, no `EMPTY_SHA256`. After the fix: the six lane unit files + `test_transitions.py` + `tests/architecture` 182 passed (`pytest-38/`); `make typecheck` clean (`typecheck-29.log`); `make lint` clean (`lint-30.log`). Landed as planned in §13.2.6, with these specifics:
- `JsonlStream(io.RawIOBase)`: `readinto` pulls one canonical line at a time into a pending buffer, so a consumer reading exactly the first line's length has made the stream produce exactly one row (pinned: `row_count == 1` after that read); `sha256`, `size`, `row_count`, `exhausted` follow the bytes handed out. `encode_rows` is the same stream read with `readall()` — one code path for the in-memory and the streaming forms; every earlier digest test still passes unchanged. The exporter passes the stream to the writer through `cast(BinaryIO, …)`, the cast `files.store` itself makes for its spooled file (a `RawIOBase` is not a `typing.BinaryIO` for mypy).
- Empty datasets: the exporter writes nothing for a dataset without rows and records `(name, EMPTY_SHA256, 0)`; the heartbeat still fires once per dataset read and once per dataset handled, so the progress cadence is unchanged. Two earlier tests moved with the rule (`verify_manifest` no longer reports the empty `customer` entry as "file missing"; the one-file-per-dataset test now expects one file per dataset **with rows** plus the manifest); recorded as consequences of the interpretation in §13.2.6, not as regressions.
- Failure detail: `fail_snapshot` adds `errors`, `written` and `failed` to the `tenant.snapshot_failed` detail **only** when the problem carries an `SNP-1-WRITE` error; every other failure keeps the two-key detail (`problem_type`, `status`) the earlier tests pin.
- `PutFileWriter` is exercised with inert fakes for `put_file` / `open_file` (agreeing read-back returns the row; a disagreeing read-back refuses "read back through open_file"); the real path — spool, encrypt, insert `file_object`, read back through the sidecar — is the store's and runs only in the DB-bound tests (written, not run).
- Contract change for inert harnesses (Codex): a byte-capture writer now reads `stream.read()`; the fakes here do exactly that and expose the stream's class name so the test can pin that datasets arrive as `JsonlStream`, not bytes.

### 13.2.8 Codex I2-R2 closure at 1cd0f7be, two wording corrections, and slice I-3.1 — I2-R3 composite-key identity (docs first)

Codex final I2-R2 / release-boundary retest at exact 1cd0f7be (PRODUCTION-F-SNP-I2-R2-RETEST-1cd0f7b.md, SHA-256 cc5797c0…, manifest f67d5386…, 27 artifacts; relayed by the supervisor): **F-SNP-I2-R2 CLOSED at the supplied-row export boundary** — both original 10-case programs 10/10; the original 23-supplement stops on the removed `nulled_as_of` observation field (raw retained; an observation-only one-key adapter yields 22/23, preserving the old optional-nulling oracle failure by design); separate unchanged-input governing checks 6/6 (run 500 excluded, count 0, audit id 500, excluded count 1, no nulling field, SUCCEEDED); stamp / Shared / verify / state-helper 11/11. No DB, storage or full-job acceptance.

Wording corrections carried into this record (supervisor):
- (a) §13.2.4 / §13.2.5 "before any write": the unstamped-release refusal comes after the `QUEUED → RUNNING` transition and the `tenant.snapshot_started` audit event, and **before any dataset or file write**; the qualifier is part of the statement from here on. The same qualifier applies to the I-3 write-error refusal ("before any manifest exists", not "before any write").
- (b) The explicit `fail_snapshot(repo, id, problem.to_json(...))` invocation in the unit tests is a **state-helper control**, not the actual dispatcher: it proves what the hook records when the registry calls it, not that the registry called it (that is the written DB-bound `FailingStore` test, not run).

**F-SNP-I2-R3 (Codex's final finding; D-98 71 refinement) — this slice.** In the original role_permission cut-parent case the helper excluded the row (count 1) but recorded `excluded_ids == [None]`: `role_permission` has no `id` column (primary key `(tenant_id, role_id, permission_code)`), and `row.get("id")` dropped the available identity. Rule: the excluded identity is the table's canonical primary key taken from the table metadata, stringified deterministically — PK column order, values as text — never None, never an invented or nulled id; `excluded_descendant_ids` and every export / manifest string carry that representation for every table; a composite-key regression sits beside the id-keyed cases.
- **Metadata fact and the reading applied.** No copied table has a single-column primary key: 71 of 73 key on `(tenant_id, id)`, `tenant_currency` on `(tenant_id, currency_code)`, `role_permission` on `(tenant_id, role_id, permission_code)`. A snapshot is one tenant's export (T-PLT-34 row, manifest stamp), so the identity is the primary key **within that scope**: the PK columns from the metadata minus `tenant_id`, in PK order, joined with `|`; a single remaining column keeps its single value (`"<id>"` for the 71 id-keyed tables — the "audit id 500" oracle stays exactly as measured; `"<role_id>|<permission_code>"` for role_permission; `"<currency_code>"` for tenant_currency). This is the only reading under which "complete primary key" and "single-column keys keep their single value" both hold; if the supervisor wants `tenant_id` repeated in every identity, it is one constant (`SCOPE_COLUMN`) — recorded, not decided by the lane.
- `snapshot_dataset.primary_key(table)`, `identity_columns(table)`, `row_identity(table, row) -> str` (a missing or NULL key column refuses with `ValueError`: never None, never invented); `apply_parent_cutoff` records `row_identity` for every excluded row and uses it in broken-reference findings; `ParentCutoff.excluded_ids` is text per table; the audit detail lists that text unchanged.
- Fail-first: pure (role_permission excluded identity `"<role_id>|contract.read"`, id-keyed single value, missing-column refusal) and handler (role → role_permission world: `excluded_descendant_ids["role_permission"]` is the PK text, never `"None"`).

### 13.2.10 Ruling D-98 75 recorded; slice I-4 — routes and OpenAPI for the snapshot job (docs first)

**Ruling D-98 75 (supervisor) — accepted as implemented in I-3:** an empty dataset is not stored (T-PLT-29 `put_file` refuses a zero-byte non-upload); its manifest entry carries `(name, sha256 of the empty content, row_count 0)` and no `file_object`; `verify_manifest` accepts a missing file **only** for an entry whose `row_count` is 0 and whose digest is the empty digest (any other missing file stays a refusal — pinned by the I-3 test with a zero-row entry carrying a non-empty digest); the load step loads nothing for it; no 04 change; the two earlier tests that moved are consequences, recorded in §13.2.7. The I2-R3 refinement the supervisor asked about is in 8e9c7bdb (slice I-3.1, §13.2.8–§13.2.9), not in fd9254ca.

**Slice I-4 (dispatched): the API of the snapshot job.** Paths: `backend/erev_api/api/v1/tenant.py` (the API-R-04 module; the route module keeps importing no handler — the pinned test), `backend/erev_api/domain/platform/snapshots.py` (new: request, list, read, manifest; no `snapshot_job` import, so collecting the route registers no handler while `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` is still in `PENDING_JOB_HANDLERS`), `backend/erev_api/schemas/tenant.py`, `docs/api/openapi.json` + `frontend/src/lib/api/schema.d.ts` (`make openapi`), `backend/tests/api/test_tenant_snapshots.py` (DB-bound, written NOT RUN), unit tests for the pure request checks and the list spec.
- **`POST /tenant/snapshots` → 202** (API-R-04; BUILD_SPEC SNP-1 body `{known_at, purpose, name}`). Guard: `command("tenant.snapshot")` — the permission is `mfa=True`, so a session without MFA gets 403 `mfa-required` from `require`; inside the handler a TOTP verification older than the BR-PLT-06 window gets 403 `mfa-step-up-required` (`mfa.step_up_fresh_at`, the approvals precedent; PRD ACT-50). Refusals by name: `validation-failed` (`known_at` later than now; a purpose outside the T-PLT-34 check; `name` longer than a label), `sandbox-restricted` (SBX-02: only a production tenant is the source — the same slug the handler uses), `forbidden`, `mfa-required`, `mfa-step-up-required`, the idempotency slugs. The handler generates the T-PLT-34 id, defers `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` with `{tenant_snapshot_id, known_at, purpose}` (subject `tenant_snapshot`), inserts the row `QUEUED` with `job_id` (not an updatable column under DB-03, so it is set at insert), writes the AUD-CMD event `tenant.snapshot_requested`, and answers like the evidence-pack precedent (04 §16.14): body API-S-Job, `Location: /api/v1/jobs/{job_id}`, header `X-Erev-Tenant-Snapshot-Id`. `name` is carried in the job params as `sandbox_name` for SNP-2's `SANDBOX_COPY` (T-PLT-34 has no name column; not stored here).
- **`GET /tenant/snapshots`** (API-R-04): `ListOut[TenantSnapshotOut]`, `require("tenant.snapshot")`; sort `-created_at` default, `created_at`, `known_at`, `id`; filters `status` (E-67 literals) and `purpose` (T-PLT-34 literals); DG-LST cursor rules through `lists.paginate`.
- **`GET /tenant/snapshots/{id}`** (status) and **`GET /tenant/snapshots/{id}/manifest`** (the stored manifest bytes verbatim, `application/json`, `ETag` = `manifest_sha256`; 404 `not-found` for an unknown id, a row of another tenant (RLS) or a snapshot without a manifest yet). **04 row request, not a lane edit:** API-R-04 lists `POST /tenant/snapshots` and `GET /tenant/snapshots` only; the supervisor's dispatch names status and manifest read, so the two `{id}` routes are implemented and this record asks the docs owner to add them to API-R-04 (`tenant.snapshot`). The manifest could also be fetched as `GET /files/{manifest_file_id}/content` once the attachment readability rule admits `SNAPSHOT_DATASET` files; the dedicated route avoids depending on that rule.
- **Out of scope, unchanged:** `SANDBOX_COPY` creating its sandbox in the same job (SNP-2 / F-LMG), `POST /tenant/sandboxes`, worker registration (P2 one-line post-merge; `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` stays in `PENDING_JOB_HANDLERS`), retention configuration (I-5), load / replay, screens.
- **Tests.** Fail-first, pure: the request checks (future `known_at`, sandbox source, purpose outside the check) as Problems by slug; the list spec's sort keys, directions and filter choices; the schema literals. Architecture (`test_dg_arc_04_*`) and `test_dg_api_10_openapi_31_document` run in the CPU suite and pin guards, command dependencies and the regenerated document. DB-bound (`tests/api/test_tenant_snapshots.py`, written NOT RUN): `test_permission_and_step_up` (BUILD_SPEC name: 403 `forbidden` without `tenant.snapshot`; 403 `mfa-step-up-required` without a fresh TOTP), `test_request_list_status_and_manifest` (202 + `Location` + header; list shows `QUEUED`; the worker run inline; status `SUCCEEDED`; the manifest bytes hash to `manifest_sha256`; 404 before completion; a sandbox source → 403 `sandbox-restricted`).

### 13.2.12 Ruling D-98 77 confirmed; Codex I-3 review at fd9254ca; slice I-4.1 — I3-R1 / I3-R2 (docs first)

**Ruling D-98 77 (clarifying the D-98 71 refinement) — confirmed as implemented in 8e9c7bdb:** the excluded identity is the row's primary key within the tenant scope the snapshot carries once (T-PLT-34 row / manifest stamp); `tenant_id` is omitted through the single `SCOPE_COLUMN`; `(tenant_id, id)` tables report the id; composite keys report the remaining PK columns in PK order joined with `|`; deterministic text; never None or invented. No change needed.

**Codex final bounded I-3 review at fd9254ca** (PRODUCTION-F-SNP-I3-REVIEW-fd9254c.md, SHA-256 757df026…, manifest 37e5680d…, 31 artifacts; relayed by the supervisor): both original ten-case programs fail first on the new `stream` keyword, then pass 10/10 each through disclosed protocol / empty-observation adapters (rows, hashes, case names, `known_at` and cutoff oracles preserved; an absent file is a logical empty only for a zero-row entry with the empty digest — D-98 75); supplement raw 40/45; twenty native stream / chunk checks match the retained pre-streaming hashes; native `PutFileWriter` with inert collaborators 3/3; empty-entry acceptance and the missing-non-empty / wrong-empty-hash refusals pass; all seven tested exporter refusal modes prevent `SUCCEEDED` and the manifest reference. No real storage / encryption / job / UOW / DB / worker acceptance. I-3.1 (8e9c7bdb) is not yet reviewed.

**I3-R1 — class-only, for every error kind.** The §13.2.6 plan said "the error's class only (DG-LOG-03)"; `_store` copied `error.detail` for a native `Problem`, so an inert Problem's detail landed in `SNP-1-WRITE.message` and the failure audit (a plain `OSError` correctly kept "OSError"). Ruling: the exception class name and nothing else, for every error kind including `Problem`; no structured details in that field. Applied: `message=type(error).__name__` unconditionally. Question returned with the exact field, not implemented: whether the catalogue slug of a `Problem` (`problem_slug`, e.g. `precondition-failed` — a catalogue identifier, not free text) may travel as a separate `ProblemError` beside the class; until ruled, a wrapped `Problem` reads "Problem".

**I3-R2 — the naming guarantee covers every refusal after the first dataset write began.** The exporter's own refusals — a stream not read to its end or a returned dataset digest that differs, and a manifest digest that differs — raised without `SNP-1-WRITTEN` / `SNP-1-WRITE`, so `fail_snapshot` omitted the written / failed names (four Codex expectations fail on deliberately corrupt writer-protocol returns). Ruling: route those branches through the same written / failed formatter; do not narrow the guarantee. Applied: one `_write_failure(detail, written, failed)` builds every post-first-write refusal; the exporter's own refusals put the refusal token `STORED_MISMATCH` in `SNP-1-WRITE.message` (a fixed identifier standing where an exception class stands — there is no exception), `failed` is the dataset name or `manifest`; `fail_snapshot` is unchanged and now sees the names in all four cases. Fail-first on Codex's failing expectations, reproduced with the fakes here (inert Problem sentinel; wrong dataset digest; partly consumed stream; wrong manifest digest).

**Carried into the record (supervisor):** the manifest is written last; in the after-write-error control, inert manifest bytes may exist in the store while no success and no manifest reference is recorded (a manifest digest mismatch, or a source-count change detected after the manifest write) — the record never claims physical absence of manifest bytes for every error; source-count verification follows the manifest write, so it is a change detector, not a transaction-consistency claim; the worker's `HANDLER_MODULES` still omits `snapshot_job` until P2's post-merge one-liner.

**I-4 note for the second dispatch text:** I-4 landed at f35c45c0 (§13.2.10–§13.2.11) before that text arrived. No new ERR / IMP slugs were introduced — every refusal reuses a catalogue slug (`validation-failed`, `sandbox-restricted`, `mfa-step-up-required`, `not-found`, `precondition-failed`), so no copy-catalogue row and no drift-pin move (0); the "read status / transitions" reading is the T-PLT-34 row (`GET /tenant/snapshots/{id}`: status, timestamps, counts, digest); the excluded-identity audit is readable through the existing audit routes (`tenant.snapshot_created` detail), not a snapshot route.
