### 13.2.14 Crossed question answered; 04 edits under provisional rev 1.19; slice I-5 — retention configuration with a refusing default (docs first)

**Answer to the supervisor's crossed question:** I3-R1 and I3-R2 are not in f35c45c0; they landed as slice I-4.1 at **e7fa41fb** (record 7bf7f256, §13.2.12–§13.2.13), reported before the question arrived.

**04 edits (ruling: lanes edit the governed documents docs-first for their own features under a provisional revision).** `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` rev **1.19 (provisional; renumbered at merge preparation)**: §15.3 API-R-04 gains `GET /tenant/snapshots/{id}` and `GET /tenant/snapshots/{id}/manifest` under `tenant.snapshot` (the I-4 routes, §13.2.10); T-PLT-31 catalogue rule 2 gains `platform.snapshot_retention_families` (below); one revision-log row. Nothing existing changes type, default, check or enumeration. The unit drift test `test_platform_parameters_match_04_table` compares the rule-2 table with `registry.platform.PLATFORM_PARAMETERS` in order, so the code spec is appended last; `test_platform_parameters_t_plt_31` pins the count 23 → 24 (a shared unit test; the pin moves with the parameter — recorded here).

**Slice I-5 (dispatched): the Q-4 retention rules as configuration with a refusing default.** The ruling on Q-4 (D-98 cand. 25) requires human confirmation (Ray / Legal) before production use; the manifest so far carried the P8 families with a `PROPOSED` status constant. Now:
- **Configuration home:** a T-PLT-31 platform parameter `platform.snapshot_retention_families` (rule 2: `allowed_levels = {TENANT}`, `pin = 'P'`, `approval_code = 'CFG'`, category PLATFORM) — the registry is the governed per-tenant setting of the platform: a value reaches a tenant only through a `registry_version` that passes the CFG approval and is PUBLISHED (T-PLT-32), which is the human confirmation the ruling asks for. Value schema: an object with the five copied families (`file_object`, `import_row`, `source_record`, `contract_event`, `manual_adjustment`) as required properties, each an enum of the four P8 retention literals (`TENANT_LIFETIME`, `AUDIT_RETENTION_YEARS`, `EXPIRES_AT_SWEEP`, `FILE_RETENTION`), no additional properties. **Default `{}`** (legacy parity `same`): nothing confirmed.
- **Refusing default:** `export_snapshot` asks the reader for the tenant's `retention_policy()`; `None` (the default, or a value that does not cover all five families) refuses with `precondition-failed` (`RETENTION_UNSET`, naming the parameter) — after `QUEUED → RUNNING` and the start audit, before the count sweep, any dataset read or any write (the unstamped-release refusal's position). A value outside the schema (unknown family or literal) refuses `validation-failed` — fail closed, never a silent fallback to the PROPOSED families.
- **Manifest:** `Retention` gains `families_status`; `retention_of(files, policy)` writes the confirmed families and `"CONFIRMED: registry version <id> (TENANT)"` in place of the PROPOSED constant; `RETENTION_FAMILIES` stays as the P8 reference the tests compare against, `RETENTION_FAMILIES_STATUS` stays as the documented PROPOSED label (no manifest carries it any more).
- **Handler:** `DbSnapshotReader(ctx, engine_release=…, retention=…)`; `tenant_snapshot_export` resolves the parameter through `registry.resolve.resolve(session, code, book_code=ASC606, known_at=params.known_at)` inside the job's unit of work — the TENANT-level PUBLISHED version in force at `known_at`, else the framework default `{}` → refusal.
- **Seeding:** `registry.platform._SPECS` gains the spec (last); migration 0003 seeds every platform parameter on a fresh database; databases already past 0003 receive the row through a provisional data revision `0100_snp_1_retention_parameter.py` (`down_revision = "0099"`; idempotent insert of that one row; downgrade deletes it) — provisional numbering as for 0099.
- **Tests.** Fail-first, pure: the spec (code, category, default `{}`, schema accepts a full mapping and rejects a partial one and an unknown literal through `versions.schema_errors`); `retention_policy_of({})` is None, a full mapping is a policy, a bad mapping refuses; the exporter with an unset policy refuses `RETENTION_UNSET` with nothing written and `QUEUED → RUNNING` only; with a policy the manifest's `retention.families` equals the policy and `families_status` starts with `CONFIRMED:`; the 04-table drift test and the count pin. DB-bound (written NOT RUN): the domain tests publish a TENANT registry version carrying the five families in the `job` fixture (`support.rows.publish_registry_version`); a new test with no published version ends the job `FAILED` with the `RETENTION_UNSET` problem and no manifest.
- Unchanged: worker registration (P2 post-merge), SNP-2 sandbox creation, load / replay.

### 13.2.16 Codex I4 review; ruling D-98 cand. 82 (`problem_slug`); slice I-5.1 (docs first)

**Codex I4 source review** (PRODUCTION-F-SNP-I4-REVIEW-4c03361.md; frozen 4c033617 / f35c45c0; 40 artifacts; relayed by the supervisor): no new route business-logic defect; two follow-ups. e7fa41fb / 7bf7f256 under separate review. The supervisor's I-5 dispatch crossed the I-5 report (761d8bbb / b308f974): the 04 API-R-04 rows and the retention configuration are done (§13.2.14–§13.2.15); this slice carries the three remaining items.

- **I4 follow-up 1 — contract coverage of the headers, at the shared layer.** The committed OpenAPI had no `Idempotency-Key` request-header schema on `POST /tenant/snapshots` and no `Location` / `X-Erev-Tenant-Snapshot-Id` / `ETag` response-header schemas, so the generated types carried `header?: never`. Plan: `api.deps` gains the shared components — `components.parameters.IdempotencyKey` (string, 8–255 characters, required; API-C-04) and `components.headers` `Location`, `ETag`, `X-Erev-Tenant-Snapshot-Id` — installed into the document by `main.create_app` (a wrapper around `app.openapi`); `GuardedRoute` adds the `$ref` parameter to every route whose dependency tree holds a `command(...)` dependency (the closures are marked at construction), so every command operation in the document declares the header once from one definition; a helper `header_responses(status, *names)` references the shared header components from a route's `responses`, applied to the four snapshot routes and the two `/tenant` settings routes (ETag). `make openapi` regenerates the document and `schema.d.ts`; `test_openapi_export` asserts the components and the four routes' header schemas (fail-first). The frontend command wrapper sends the header itself (`commands.ts`), so a required header parameter changes no client call; `tsc` runs inside `make typecheck`.
- **I4 follow-up 2 — wording.** "before any row exists" → "before job / snapshot creation" in §13.2.11 (applied above); the code carried no such comment. The command wrapper's idempotency claim and the denied-permission audit event are preserved exactly.
- **Ruling D-98 cand. 82 — `problem_slug`, admitted.** A wrapped catalogue `Problem` carries its slug in a separate `ProblemError` whose `field` is `problem_slug` (`rule_id` `SNP-1-WRITE-SLUG`, `message` = the slug), validated against the catalogue at construction (`ValueError` otherwise; never free text, never the detail); `SNP-1-WRITE.message` stays the class name; a non-catalogue exception yields the class only. Reading recorded: "separate ProblemError field `problem_slug`" is implemented as a separate `ProblemError` entry keyed by `field`, not a new attribute on `ProblemError` (which would change every problem document and the `ProblemOut` schema).

### 13.2.18 Crossed dispatches consolidated; Codex F-SNP-I41-R1 — ruling D-98 candidate 84 (docs first)

**Consolidation.** The supervisor's I-5a dispatch (header schemas + wording + `problem_slug`) and the "context only" note on Codex's I4.1 retest (e7fa41fb / 7bf7f256: original streaming program 45/45, both cutoff programs 10/10; I3-R1 / R2 closed for the original five findings) crossed the I-5.1 report: I-5a is done at 84d3be03 / 27ca8775 (§13.2.16–§13.2.17). The Codex retest targets outstanding are 8e9c7bdb, 761d8bbb and 84d3be03; the commit of this slice is the target for I41-R1.

**F-SNP-I41-R1** (PRODUCTION-F-SNP-I41-COUNT-SUPPLEMENT-7bf7f25.md; separately labelled inert case, 5/6; relayed by the supervisor): at e7fa41fb — and unchanged through 84d3be03 — a source-count change detected after manifest storage raises a direct `Problem`: the refusal type (`precondition-failed`), the absence of `SUCCEEDED` and of a manifest reference, and the `FAILED` end are right, but the failure audit carries only `problem_type` / `status` — `written` is absent although the stored datasets are known, because `fail_snapshot` adds `written` only when it sees `SNP-1-WRITE` and this refusal has no failed write.

**Ruling D-98 candidate 84 (cited).** Every refusal raised after the first dataset write began records the datasets stored so far (`SNP-1-WRITTEN`, write order) whether or not a write failed. A refusal caused by a post-write validation step carries no `SNP-1-WRITE` — never an invented failed dataset or phase token — and instead a fixed identifier `SNP-1-CHECK` naming the check (`source_count` here; any later post-write check uses its own name), with the class-only message per I3-R1. Refusal type, no `SUCCEEDED` / manifest reference and the `FAILED` transition unchanged.

**Helper promise (supersedes the §13.2.12 wording "the datasets already stored and the failed dataset").** After the first dataset write began, every refusal carries `SNP-1-WRITTEN` = the datasets stored so far in write order; a refusal tied to a dataset or manifest write carries `SNP-1-WRITE` (field = that dataset or `manifest`; message = the error class, or the refusal token `STORED_MISMATCH` where no exception exists; plus `SNP-1-WRITE-SLUG` for a wrapped catalogue `Problem`, D-98 cand. 82); a refusal raised by a post-write check carries `SNP-1-CHECK` (field = the check name, message = the class name `Problem`) and no `SNP-1-WRITE`. `fail_snapshot` records `errors` and `written` whenever `SNP-1-WRITTEN` is present, `failed` when `SNP-1-WRITE` is, `check` when `SNP-1-CHECK` is, `problem_slug` when `SNP-1-WRITE-SLUG` is.

**Plan.** `snapshot_job._check_failure(detail, written, check)` builds the post-write-check refusal; the `source_untouched` findings after the manifest write raise through it with check `source_count`; `fail_snapshot` keys its detail on the rule ids as above. Fail-first with Codex's one case reproduced with the fakes as a separate test (the second `counts()` reports `ssp_book` 1 → 2 after a world with `product`; expected `written = [product, ssp_book, ssp_book_version, ssp_entry, ssp_range]`, `SNP-1-CHECK` = `source_count`, no `SNP-1-WRITE`; the row ends `FAILED` through the state helper); the earlier fake test that pinned the two-key detail for this refusal moves to the structured detail (a lane test; Codex's 65-result programs are untouched). 04 and dev-guide do not enumerate the `SNP-1-*` metadata fields, so no governed-document edit beyond this record. Captured statuses as in §13.2.16; the release selection re-run from a clean commit context.

### 13.2.20 Codex I5 validation; F-SNP-I5-Q1 — ruling D-98 candidate 86; slice I-5.3 (docs first)

**Crossed dispatch.** The supervisor's I41-R1 dispatch arrived after ff3a735e / b68c56df (§13.2.18–§13.2.19) had closed it. The release-selection gate is the repo file `docs/reviews/loop/sprint/rg-selection.txt` (220 ids), not `.run/supervisor/rg-selection.txt` (174); the 174/174 runs stand as recorded (development mode / clean context respectively) and the 220 selection is run from this slice on.

**Codex I5 validation retest at 761d8bbb / b308f974** (PRODUCTION-F-SNP-I5-RETENTION-REVIEW-b308f97.md; relayed): 40 approved controls pass; no real retention / shredding / legal-hold claim; SQL compiled, not executed. **F-SNP-I5-Q1:** the plan requires human confirmation (Ray / Legal) of the families, but a PUBLISHED CFG registry version does not evidence it — the lifecycle permits `AUTO_APPROVAL`, the approvals engine auto-publishes, `published_by` may be None — while the manifest says CONFIRMED.

**Ruling D-98 candidate 86 (cited).** The confirming registry version must carry a named human approval: `AUTO_APPROVAL` is disallowed for `platform.snapshot_retention_families` (approval CFG with a required human approver); the approver identity and instant are recorded; `published_by` is non-null and no auto-publish path applies; the manifest's `families_status` reads "CONFIRMED: registry version <id> approved by <approver> at <instant>" from that record; an in-force version lacking such a record refuses by name **`RETENTION_UNCONFIRMED`** before any count / read / write (beside `RETENTION_UNSET`). The retention periods themselves remain Ray / Legal (candidate 25 stays open); this rules only how the prerequisite is evidenced — relabelling alone does not close it.

**Plan.**
- **Registry-side rule.** `registry.platform.HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED = frozenset({"platform.snapshot_retention_families"})`; the registry-version submit command passes `auto_approval=False` to `approvals.engine.submit` when the version's values carry any such code (`registry_versions.auto_approval_allowed(values)`), so no `AUTO_APPROVAL` rule can match and the request waits for a human decision (the engine's existing `auto_approval` flag, BUILD_SPEC CTR-9 precedent). Unit-tested on the pure predicate and the constant.
- **Evidence check (pure).** `snapshot_dataset.retention_confirmation(version, decisions) -> RetentionConfirmation(approver_id, decided_at)` requires `published_by` non-null, `approval_request_id` non-null and at least one `approval_decision` with `decision = APPROVE`, `approver_kind = USER`, `approver_id` non-null and no `auto_rule_id` (an `AUTO_APPROVE` decision never counts); the latest such decision names the approver (the `app_user` id — not a name or e-mail, PRV-01) and the instant; anything else raises `UnconfirmedRetention(reason)`. `RetentionPolicy.source` becomes "registry version <id> approved by <approver> at <instant>" so `families_status` reads exactly as ruled.
- **Wrapper.** `snapshot_job.retention_policy_for` loads the resolved version row and its decisions, applies the check, and returns `RetentionResolution(policy, refusal)`; `DbSnapshotReader.retention_policy()` raises `precondition-failed` `RETENTION_UNCONFIRMED` (with the reason) when a refusal is set — at the exporter's existing retention step: after `QUEUED → RUNNING` and the start audit, before the count sweep, any dataset read or any write. `RETENTION_UNSET` (no in-force value) is unchanged.
- **Tests, fail-first on ff3a735e.** Pure: a human-approved version confirms with approver and instant; an `AUTO_APPROVE` decision refuses; `published_by` None refuses; no decision refuses. Exporter (fakes): an unconfirmed reader refuses `RETENTION_UNCONFIRMED` with `counts()` never called, nothing written, `QUEUED → RUNNING` only; the confirmed manifest reads "CONFIRMED: registry version … approved by … at …". Registry: the constant and the predicate. DB-bound (written NOT RUN): the domain and API tests confirm retention with a human APPROVE decision and `published_by`; a new test with an auto-approved version ends the job FAILED with `RETENTION_UNCONFIRMED`.
- **Governed documents:** `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` — the T-PLT-31 parameter row and the revision-log entry, both under the existing provisional rev 1.19 (no new provisional row). Captured statuses incl. the 220 selection.

### 13.2.24 Codex I5.3 source review — I53-S1 / I53-S2, two written-fixture defects; slice I-5.5 (docs first)

**Codex I5.3 source review at 248e4dfd / dd65a4b6** (PRODUCTION-F-SNP-I53-SOURCE-REVIEW-dd65a4b.md; relayed): the production wiring is confirmed — `lifecycle.submit` passes `auto_approval=False` for retention-bearing versions; the wrapper checks named USER evidence before counts / copies / writes. Two source-predicted defects in the written (NOT RUN) DB tests:
- **I53-S1.** `tests/api/test_tenant_snapshots.py` still confirmed retention with `publish_registry_version` alone — a factory that supplies neither a decision nor `published_by` — while the API test expects `SUCCEEDED` and a manifest; §13.2.21 wrongly said the API helper followed the new evidence pattern (corrected above).
- **I53-S2.** The domain fixture called that factory (request already `APPROVED`) and then inserted an `approval_decision`: the DB-10 trigger of migration 0013 (`BEFORE INSERT` on `approval_decision`) requires the request `PENDING` with the decision's content hash, the step `ACTIVE` and belonging to the request, an approver who is not the preparer, has no earlier decision and holds an `ACTIVE` membership — so the sequence would be refused; and its AUTO branch supplied a non-null approver without an auto rule, contradicting the AUTO shape.

**Plan (slice I-5.5; still written NOT RUN — no lane database).** One lane-owned support helper `tests/support/snapshots.py::confirm_retention(session, tenant_id, *, at, human=True)` builds the evidence in the admitted order and is used by both test files: the version walks `DRAFT → TESTED → SUBMITTED` with one content hash (the factory's own sequence); the approval request is inserted `PENDING` for subject `REGISTRY_VERSION` with that hash and an `ACTIVE` step 1; the human `APPROVE` decision follows (approver = an `ACTIVE` member of the tenant, never the SYSTEM preparer; the request's hash; `mfa_verified_at` set); then step `ACTIVE → APPROVED`, request `PENDING → APPROVED` (the DB-03 pairs), version `APPROVED → PUBLISHED` with `published_at` and `published_by` = the approver. `human=False` keeps the legacy auto-publish residue exactly as an auto path leaves it — the request `APPROVED` with no decision at all and `published_by` NULL — which `retention_confirmation` refuses on `published_by` ("no auto-publish path confirms retention"); a correctly shaped `AUTO_APPROVE` decision row (approver NULL, `auto_rule_set_version_id` and `auto_rule_id` set) needs rule-set fixtures this lane does not build, and the pure test already pins that shape's refusal. Success expectations of the domain and API tests are kept; no trigger is disabled and no rule relaxed. The API test also confirms retention before its worker run (S1). Captured CPU statuses (typecheck, lint, focused lane selection, full CPU, 220 selection); the commit is Codex's I5.3-fixture target; Codex's separate retest plan (PRODUCTION-F-SNP-I53-RETEST-PLAN-dd65a4b.md, SHA 180b4956…) runs against the frozen source; candidate 25 stays open.

### 13.4.3 Codex packet 1154 (a9fb60fe) — F-SNP-PREP-S1 (written-test defect) and F-SNP-PREP-S2 → ruling D-98 candidate 106 (docs first)

**PREP-S1.** `tests/pg/test_snapshot_retention_evidence.py` (and the three negative cases of `tests/domain/platform/test_snapshots.py` that share the pattern) call `registry.run_inline`, which calls the handler directly with `persisted=False` and neither catches the refusal nor invokes `on_failure`; the intended `RETENTION_UNCONFIRMED` / `RETENTION_UNSET` / write-error refusals therefore escape as a `Problem` before any `FAILED` assertion runs. Fix the tests, not the guard: at the inline boundary assert the specific native refusal — `pytest.raises(Problem)` with slug `precondition-failed` and the exact `RETENTION_UNCONFIRMED` (naming `AUTO_APPROVE` for the auto case) / `RETENTION_UNSET` shape; keep the positive human-manifest case; add a separate **persisted-dispatch** case for the FAILED lifecycle that is claimed — `insert_job` → `dispatch` → the task marked fetched → `run_job` (the jobs API test's pattern), asserting the job row `FAILED` with the problem and the T-PLT-34 row `FAILED` through `on_failure`. "Nothing was read or written" is observed explicitly and narrowly: no `file_object` of purpose `SNAPSHOT_DATASET` for the tenant, no `tenant.snapshot_created` audit event, `manifest_file_id` NULL — not a claim about every earlier SQL statement. Still pg-marked, NOT RUN.

**PREP-S2 → ruling D-98 candidate 106 (cited).** `migration_batch.import_upload_ids` is an ordered UUID array (T-MIG-01, REPLAY order of legacy uploads); the reference scanner handled scalar `*_id` columns only and export copied the array unchanged — declared coverage today (the column is always `{}`), not a current bug. Policy, as ruled: an **array reference** declares its element target table (and thus class) in `ARRAY_REFERENCES`; export applies the scalar rule per element, preserving order — an element is kept when its target row is in the copied set, dropped when the target is REGENERATED or cut by `known_at` (the drops are counted per column in the manifest, `array_drops`, and in the created audit detail), and refused by name when it names no source row (a broken reference, `validation-failed`, before any write); the scanner covers array columns; the inventory drift guard refuses an undeclared array reference. Scope of the declaration: every UUID-element array of a copied table — `gl_account.entity_ids` and `role_assignment.entity_ids` → `legal_entity`, `event_submission.applied_event_ids` and `estimate_version.applied_event_ids` → `contract_event`, `contract_event.obligation_ids` and `estimate.target_obligation_ids` → `obligation`, `migration_batch.import_upload_ids` → `import_upload`; text arrays (`required_dimensions`, `function_*_permissions`, `codification_refs`, `flags`) are not references and `applied_invoice_external_ids` (external identifiers) joins `NON_REFERENCES`. Array references are filtered per element, never nulled and never a load-order (parent-first) constraint — the database declares no FK on them. Tests: kept / cut / absent per element with the column non-empty in a fixture (pure) and the manifest's `array_drops` through the handler (fakes); the undeclared-array guard. 04: the T-MIG-01 column note under rev 1.47 (this commit). The C2 retention-family question stays with Ray (cand. 25).

### 13.4.5 Codex packet 1241 — SCOPE-1 / SCOPE-2 in the 106 implementation; ruling D-98 candidate 106a; slice FSNP-SCOPE-1 (docs first)

**Findings (source-derived, at b94828d0).** SCOPE-1: `gl_account.entity_ids` → `legal_entity` was filtered by the generic per-element rule while `[]` means *all* entities (04 T-REF-13; `accounts.py`), and `commands.py` lets `entity_ids` change without touching `created_at` — an account created before `known_at`, later scoped to an entity created after it, would be kept with `[]` and silently expanded to every surviving entity; the `array_drops` count does not preserve the scope. SCOPE-2: `role_assignment.entity_ids` under the same rule, where 0007's `CHECK (is_all_entities = (cardinality(entity_ids) = 0))` makes a scoped row filtered to `[]` with `is_all_entities` false unloadable (no direct escalation — `permissions.py` reads the boolean — but the flag is never to be repaired by flipping).

**Ruling D-98 candidate 106a (cited).** Scope arrays are declared in the registry: `SCOPE_ARRAYS` with `empty_means_all` for `gl_account.entity_ids` and `paired_flag = "is_all_entities"` for `role_assignment.entity_ids`. A partial cut keeps the remaining non-empty scope — the excluded targets do not exist in the copy, so the effective scope over the surviving entities is unchanged — counted in `array_drops`; a **complete** cut of a non-empty scope array is a refusal by name **`SNAPSHOT_SCOPE_UNREPRESENTABLE`** (table, column, row identity, cut targets), raised before any write; the copy never turns a restriction into universal applicability and never flips the paired flag; rows empty at the source keep `[]` and their flag; unchanged rows are unchanged. Scalar / replay / reference populations and the negative-test boundaries of b94828d0 are untouched.

**Plan.** `snapshot_dataset.ScopeRule(empty_means_all, paired_flag)` + `SCOPE_ARRAYS`; `apply_parent_cutoff` consults it after the per-element filter: a non-empty source array filtered to `[]` on a scope column is recorded in `ParentCutoff.unrepresentable` (finding "`<table>.<column>: <identity> — every scoped target cut (<ids>); [] would mean all entities`") and the row is left as read (never rewritten to `[]`); the exporter refuses `validation-failed` with rule `SNAPSHOT_SCOPE_UNREPRESENTABLE` (field `table.column`, message = identity + cut targets) right after the broken-reference check, before selection and any write. Docs: the two 04 column notes (T-PLT-10, T-REF-13) under rev 1.47 (this commit). Fail-first: pure unchanged / partial / complete on both columns, the load-CHECK invariant `is_all_entities == (len(entity_ids) == 0)` over every exported role_assignment row, the flag never flipped; handler: a complete cut refuses by name with nothing written and `QUEUED → RUNNING` only.

### 13.4.14 Correction plan — pre-seeded `registry_parameter` metadata (Codex 0226 rollout gap; checkpoint requested by Codex 0354 through the supervisor)

**Gap, restated.** `registry_parameter` is an append-only global catalogue (IM-A, `global_reference`; DB-01 refuses UPDATE and DELETE; primary key `code`). 0003 creates the table and seeds it with `insert_rows` (no ON CONFLICT); 0063 appends its one row with `ON CONFLICT (code) DO NOTHING`; the rollout diagnosis does not depend on both using ON CONFLICT (precision per Codex 0408). A database seeded before 7395e72e keeps the old `value_schema` (all five families required, `{}` invalid) in its `platform.snapshot_retention_families` row, while the Python catalogue now admits `{}`. Create / submit / publish validate against `registry.PARAMETERS` (Python), so the acceptance fix works in source; the catalogue API (`list_parameters`, `select(registry_parameter)`) advertises the PERSISTED schema, so such a database advertises stale metadata. **Production compatibility is not claimed until the forward correction lands.** Fresh databases (`erev_test`, lane databases, a first production database created from 0003 at or after this head) are unaffected.

**Chosen design (to implement as the next bounded slice; nothing written yet — numbers on request):**
1. **An append-only supersession table the reader honours** — working name `registry_parameter_correction` (T-PLT-xx, number on request): `code text FK → registry_parameter(code)`, `correction_no integer CHECK (>= 1)`, the correctable metadata columns (`value_schema`, `description`, `source_ref`, `section` — never `pin`, `approval_code`, `allowed_levels`, `category`, the defaults or the forced flags, which change behaviour and belong to a new parameter code), `applied_by_revision text` (the Alembic revision that appended it), SC-C; primary key `(code, correction_no)`; class IM-A `global_reference` (append-only like its parent; DB-01 on UPDATE / DELETE); no tenant column (RLS-NONE-G like `registry_parameter`). This appends rather than updates, respects the append-only registry, and keeps every advertised text auditable (the seeded row and each correction remain readable).
2. **Read contract:** `list_parameters` (and any reader of parameter metadata) advertises `COALESCE(latest correction, seeded row)` per metadata column — the latest `correction_no` per code wins; behavioural columns always come from the seeded row (and the Python catalogue governs validation as today). 04: the T-PLT-31 note and the catalogue API row state the contract; dev-guide DG-MIG-07 gains the sentence "a metadata correction to a seeded `registry_parameter` row is appended as a `registry_parameter_correction` row by a new Alembic revision; the seeded row is never updated" (mirroring the report-definition versioning already in DG-MIG-07).
3. **Forward Alembic step** — `0066` if assigned (main is at 0065; coordinated with the supervisor and F-LMG before writing): creates the table, its class and grants, and appends `('platform.snapshot_retention_families', 1, <the current Python rendering of the value_schema>, …, '0066')`. Its `downgrade()` drops the table (it created it — DG-MIG-04 proper; no append-only row is deleted by hand). No UPDATE, no trigger bypass, no reset.
4. **Guards:** CPU — the 0066 literal equals `json.dumps(PLATFORM_PARAMETERS[code].value_schema)` (the drift oracle, as `test_revision_seed_equals_fresh_rendering` does for report definitions), and a pure test that the read contract's COALESCE picks the latest correction; DB-bound (written, NOT RUN in the lane) — after `upgrade head`, the advertised `value_schema` of every code equals the Python catalogue's (`test_registry_parameter_seeded` extended to the advertised view), and the round trip drops the table cleanly.
5. **Alternatives rejected:** (a) updating the seeded row through an allow-listed UPDATE (the `file_object` IM-A precedent) — contradicts DG-MIG-08's rationale and would let a seeded text change without a trace; (b) a read-time overlay of the Python catalogue without persistence — the advertised metadata would no longer be a persisted, auditable fact and the seed drift oracle would have to accept a permanent mismatch; (c) a direct DB UPDATE or trigger bypass — excluded by the review's authority and by DB-01.

**Touches and numbers:** 04 under the assigned, unlanded **1.56** (new T-PLT-xx table row — number on request; T-PLT-31 note; §14.2 grants row; §15 catalogue API read contract; §18 rule 2 first appearance) — or a fresh 04 number if the supervisor prefers the metadata table separated from 1.56's landed content; dev-guide DG-MIG-07 sentence under **1.48** (assigned, unlanded) or a fresh number; Alembic **0066** on request. Blockers: none for READY; the slice waits for the numbers and for the confirmation that a new table is the preferred mechanism (the supervisor may instead rule a catalogue-version bump).

### 13.4.16 T-PLT-47 `registry_parameter_correction` / Alembic 0066 — the forward correction of pre-seeded catalogue metadata (D-98 candidate 125; Codex 0226 / 0408; supervisor's numbers 04 1.59, dev-guide 1.53, T-PLT-47, 0066)

**Gap closed at source (production compatibility still not claimed until this lands and the next integrated batch measures the DB-bound cases).** `registry_parameter` is append-only with primary key `code`; 0003 seeds it with `insert_rows` and 0063 appends its row with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so a database seeded before 04 1.56 advertises the all-five-required `value_schema` of `platform.snapshot_retention_families` through `GET /registry/parameters` although create / submit / publish validate the Python catalogue. The correction is appended, never updated, and read through ONE effective relation.

**Docs first — d4ab4f05 + d13e152f** (`make lint` rc=0, `lint-119.log`, `lint-120.log`): 04 **1.59** — new T-PLT-47 section (Purpose / Class IM-A global reference, RLS-NONE-G, `erev_app` SELECT only / REQ areas / Rev bullet; columns `code` FK → `registry_parameter (code)`, `correction_no` ≥ 1, the four metadata columns NOT NULL as complete snapshots, `applied_by_revision`, SC-C; keys `PRIMARY KEY (code, correction_no)` — the redundant DESC index first written was removed in d13e152f because the primary key serves the latest-correction read and `_index_sql` admits identifiers only); T-PLT-31 Rev 1.59 bullet + catalogue rule 3 (effective row = latest correction COALESCEd over the seed per metadata column; behavioural columns from the seed; validation on the Python catalogue; one relation for list / filter / search / pagination); §2 ERD edge; §4 PLT area entry 47; API-R-13 read-contract sentence; §14.2 global-reference grant row names the table; §18 rule 12 first appearance by **0066** on **0065**. dev-guide **1.53** — DG-MIG-07 metadata-correction sentence. Header cells and 4- / 5-cell log rows; widths checked (the two pre-existing 7-cell dev-guide rows 1.28 / 1.45 are other lanes').

**Code — 15670423 (+ ae08e6d0, a docstring word):**
1. **Alembic `0066_registry_parameter_correction.py`** (`revision = "0066"`, `down_revision = "0065"`): `create_global_table` (columns as 04; PK `(code, correction_no)`; `standard_sets=["SC-C"]`; `ck_registry_parameter_correction__correction_no`), `add_global_fk(code → registry_parameter.code)`, `apply_class(IM-A, global_reference=True)` (SELECT-only grant, DB-01 UPDATE / DELETE triggers, class marker), then `insert_rows` of correction 1 — `CODE`, `1`, `VALUE_SCHEMA`, `DESCRIPTION`, `SOURCE_REF`, `SECTION`, `'0066'`, `'SYSTEM'` — as module LITERALS rendered from the Python catalogue at authoring time (DG-MIG-12; no registry import); `downgrade()` = `drop_global_table` only. No seeded row is touched.
2. **`erev_api/registry/effective.py`** (kernel): `LATEST_CORRECTION` = `SELECT DISTINCT ON (code) … ORDER BY code, correction_no DESC` over T-PLT-47; `EFFECTIVE_REGISTRY_PARAMETER` = seed `LEFT OUTER JOIN` latest with `coalesce(latest.x, seed.x) AS x` for exactly the four metadata columns and every behavioural column from the seed; one row per code. Complete snapshots make COALESCE equal "latest row wins" (04 T-PLT-47).
3. **Readers bind the effective relation, never the seed** (Codex 0408): `registry_versions.list_parameters` selects it; `api/v1/policies.PARAMETER_LIST` binds `sort_keys` (`id`, `code`), the `category` / `section` filters and the `q` search columns (`code`, `description`) to it — so pagination totals and cursors, filters and search follow a correction. `RegistryParameterOut` unchanged; no OpenAPI change.
4. **Registrations:** ORM table in `db/tables/platform.py` (placed after `_sc_c()`; columns in 04 order — `test_dg_arc_09_table_columns_match` green); `db/lint.py` `GLOBAL_TABLES`; `snapshot_dataset.RULES` SHARED global (pin 8 → 9 — "SHARED is exactly the tables without tenant_id"); `privacy/classification.py` `_table("registry_parameter_correction", "T-PLT-47", …)` in 04 order (literal-format pin 153 → 154). New code, so no fail-first against a batch defect — the guards pin the design (the batch's own evidence is the Codex 0226 diagnosis).

### 14.2 Design note (sent to the supervisor before code; rulings requested where marked ▶)

**Scope (BUILD_SPEC SNP-2; 05 §10 SBX-02 to SBX-06, SBX-11; 04 T-PLT-01 `source_tenant_id` / `source_known_at`, §14.1 DB-05 / DB-08 / DB-15, §15.4 `SANDBOX_DETERMINISM_MISMATCH`; 05 RCP-19; REQ-PLT-023).** Two entry points share one loader `backend/erev_api/domain/platform/sandboxes.py::load_sandbox`: (a) `POST /tenant/snapshots` with purpose `SANDBOX_COPY` (and `SANDBOX_SEED`) — the `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` job exports and then loads its sandbox in the same job (BUILD_SPEC: "creates its sandbox in the same job"); (b) `POST /tenant/sandboxes` (API-R-04; 202 API-S-Job; body `{tenant_snapshot_id, name}`) — restores a STORED, `SUCCEEDED` snapshot of the current tenant into a NEW sandbox. No schema change; no engine change; `make openapi` for the new route.

**Job and subject.** No new E-14 kind: (a) is the existing `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` job continuing after its manifest; (b) defers a `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` job in **load-only** mode — params `{tenant_snapshot_id, load: {sandbox_tenant_id, name, requested_by}}`; the handler refuses to re-export a `SUCCEEDED` row and runs only the load. The sandbox tenant id is **pre-allocated by the request** (`new_id()`), returned in the 202 body / header (`X-Erev-Sandbox-Tenant-Id`, the §16.14 precedent), and is the job's T-PLT-13 subject: `subject_type = 'tenant'`, `subject_id` = the sandbox tenant (the row does not exist yet at enqueue time — `job.subject_id` carries no FK; the load creates it). ▶ Ruling asked: load-only mode of `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` vs a new `job_kind` value (an E-14 enum change with a revision); this note proposes the former.

**Actor and visibility (04 1.56).** The DB-15 trigger reads `erev.tenant` as the invoker under RLS-TN — current tenant, ACTIVE memberships of `current_user_id()`, or the read-only `tenant_directory` scope; provisioning reveals nothing. The job runs under the SOURCE tenant context (the `tenant_snapshot` row is RLS-T to the source), so the sandbox row is visible for the `target_tenant_id` write only through the requesting user's membership of the sandbox. Sequence: (1) in `provisioning` scope create the sandbox `tenant` row with the pre-allocated id, `kind = 'sandbox'`, `source_tenant_id`, `source_known_at = known_at`, `code` derived from `name` (refusal `sandbox-name-taken` on collision), its `audit_chain_head` and audit key — no roles / books / chain rows beyond what the datasets carry; (2) under the SANDBOX tenant context load the datasets in `LOAD_ORDER` (memberships and role assignments among them — the requester's ACTIVE membership of the source becomes an ACTIVE membership of the sandbox); (3) under `DbContext(tenant_id = source, user_id = requested_by)` set `tenant_snapshot.target_tenant_id = sandbox` — the trigger now sees a `sandbox`-kind row through the membership. A request without a user principal (SYSTEM / operator) is refused by name (`sandbox-actor-required`) before any write: the target could never become visible. `test_db_15_snapshot_target_must_be_sandbox` (landed) is the DB witness of the rule; SNP-2 adds the job-path witness.

**Datasets consumed as landed (no registry change).** `RULES` classes: COPIED rows stream in `LOAD_ORDER` from the manifest's JSONL files (SNP-1's canonical `encode_stream` serialisation; `tenant_id` — `SCOPE_COLUMN` — is omitted in the files and re-stamped to the sandbox on insert; every other column, including the row identity, is preserved, so intra-copy references stay valid and SHARED identities travel by `SHARED_KEYS`); REGENERATED datasets are not loaded — they are recomputed; PENDING / EXCLUDED_SECRET targets were NULLed at export by `REBUILD_STEPS` (`None`) and stay NULL; `"recompute"` steps (`contract.latest_computation_id`, `combination_group.head_computation_id`, `manual_adjustment.subledger_posting_id`) are filled by the sandbox recompute; `TOKEN_COLUMNS` (F-LMG, landed) are copied as opaque values; `SCOPE_ARRAYS` as read; `CUTOFF_RULES` were applied at export (the files already hold the cut). Streams: `contract_event` rows are inserted in source `record_seq` order; DB-08 assigns new `recorded_at` / `record_seq` (the copied facts are "known at copy time"; `tenant.source_known_at` records the source instant); `contract.head_stream_version` is copied as a fact and must equal the last inserted `stream_version` per contract (a load-time check). Every dataset file's SHA-256 and row count are verified against the manifest BEFORE its rows are inserted (`snapshot-digest-mismatch`); the manifest's `Stamp` (engine release, source tenant, `known_at`, purpose) and `format_version` are checked against the running registry (`snapshot-registry-mismatch`: a manifest whose dataset list, identity columns or format differ from the running `snapshot_dataset` registry is not loaded — the registry is versioned by the release stamp, not guessed).

**Periods, recompute, replay (SBX-04).** All periods load `open` (the `period_state` / `period_state_transition` / `period_lock` REPLAY_REFERENCE datasets are not inserted; `period_state` rows are opened through the normal opening for every copied `period`). Then every `combination_group` is recomputed with `compute_group(uow, group_id, trigger=ComputationTrigger.MIGRATION)` in its own transaction (TXN-08), inside the load job; ▶ the RCP-19 fan-out (child `CONTRACT_COMPUTE` jobs, chunks of 250) is the close path's mechanism — this note proposes in-job sequential recompute with the job's budget for SNP-2 and names fan-out as the follow-up if a world exceeds the budget. Then the landed pure `snapshot_replay.replay_plan` orders the source's transitions and locks; each step is executed through the normal close commands under the sandbox context with the copied approval evidence (approval requests / decisions referenced by the copied `period_lock` rows travel with the copy; the copied `approval_request_id` on the lock is the re-executed request); "closed periods receive their released postings before transition" = the journal release for a closing period runs before its transition, as the close commands require; `verify_end_state` proves the reached states and last locks equal the source's. ▶ A transition the normal command refuses in the sandbox (a gate the copied evidence does not satisfy) is recorded as a WARNING exception item `SANDBOX_REPLAY_BLOCKED` (▶ new 04 §15.4 code) with the period left in its last reachable state, the load still `SUCCEEDED` with the manifest naming the blocked periods — versus failing the load; this note proposes the WARNING because the copy is still a usable sandbox and the mismatch is visible, and asks for the ruling.

**Determinism (SBX-05).** What is hashed: nothing new — the engine's `output_sha256` per (`combination_group_id`, `book_code`) of the sandbox's recomputed versions, compared with the SOURCE's latest version as of `known_at` (landed pure `snapshot_export.latest_as_of` / `compare_determinism` / `DeterminismReport`; source versions are read from the source tenant in one repeatable-read transaction at load time — versions are REGENERATED data and not in the copy). "Load twice → identical" means: two loads of the same stored snapshot produce sandboxes whose COPIED datasets are identical row for row (same identities, same values, `tenant_id` aside), whose recomputed `output_sha256` agree per pair with each other and with the source, and whose regenerated tokens (computation / version / trace ids, `recorded_at` / `record_seq`, job ids, the sandbox tenant id) differ by construction and are therefore excluded from the comparison; NULLed pending / secret references are NULL in both. `derived_mismatches` = the count of pairs whose hashes differ (or where the sandbox recomputed nothing for a pair the source has); a non-zero count raises ONE exception item `SANDBOX_DETERMINISM_MISMATCH` (WARNING; `raise_exception_item` with `source = ENGINE`, dedupe on the snapshot id) in the sandbox and notifies the requester (`events.notifications.notify`). ▶ Where the count is recorded: SBX-05 says "in the snapshot manifest"; the manifest is written and hashed (`manifest_sha256`) at export, before any load, and T-PLT-34 has no `derived_mismatches` column. Proposal: for (a) SANDBOX_COPY the manifest is written AFTER verification (export → load → verify → manifest), so `derived_mismatches` is a manifest field; for (b) a stored-backup restore the manifest is immutable, so the load writes a **load report** file (purpose `SNAPSHOT_DATASET`, `{sandbox_tenant_id, derived_mismatches, mismatches[], blocked_periods[], loaded_at}`) whose id and SHA-256 the `tenant.snapshot_loaded` audit event carries — no T-PLT-34 change. Alternative: a nullable `derived_mismatches` / `load_report_file_id` on T-PLT-34 (04 + revision 0068). Ruling asked.

**Named refusals (problems; 04 §15.2 rows on request):** `snapshot-not-loadable` (row not `SUCCEEDED`, no manifest, purpose `STORED_BACKUP` requested through (a)), `snapshot-registry-mismatch`, `snapshot-digest-mismatch`, `sandbox-actor-required`, `sandbox-name-taken`, `snapshot-target-not-sandbox` (DB-15 `EREV-SBX-001` surfaced when the trigger refuses — defence in depth behind the actor rule), `not-production` (the existing refusal of a copy FROM a sandbox stays). WARNING exception items, not refusals: `SANDBOX_DETERMINISM_MISMATCH` (exists, 04 §15.4-B) and ▶ `SANDBOX_REPLAY_BLOCKED` (new).

**Audit (SBX-06).** The sandbox's FIRST audit event `tenant.snapshot_loaded`: source tenant id, `known_at`, manifest SHA-256, row counts, the source's `audit_chain_head` at `known_at` (the last source `audit_event` hmac with `created_at ≤ known_at`, read at load time), the load report id / SHA-256 when one exists, `derived_mismatches`. The source tenant already records `tenant.snapshot_created` at export (SNP-1); the restore path (b) adds `tenant.sandbox_restored` in the source (▶ 04 §16 audit catalogue rows: `tenant.snapshot_loaded`, `tenant.sandbox_restored`; PRV-01 catalogue entries for any new stored object). `audit_history_carried` stays false: no source audit events are copied.

**Isolation (SBX-11; DB witnesses, written NOT RUN):** `tests/pg/test_sandbox_isolation.py::test_sandbox_session_cannot_write_source` (a sandbox session appending an event with the source's tenant id fails by RLS); source row counts and the source `audit_chain_head` unchanged by a copy (the landed `test_source_unchanged_and_audited` extends to the load); `tenant.kind` and `source_tenant_id` immutability (DB-05) on the sandbox row.

**Governed-document rows foreseen (numbers on request at the docs-first step):** 04 — API-R-04 (`POST /tenant/sandboxes` body / 202 / `X-Erev-Sandbox-Tenant-Id`; the load-only job), §15.2 the six new problem slugs, §15.4-B `SANDBOX_REPLAY_BLOCKED` (if ruled), §16 audit actions (`tenant.snapshot_loaded`, `tenant.sandbox_restored`), T-PLT-34 note on the load report (or the alternative columns), §14.3-style note on sandbox creation by the load (no seed beyond the datasets); 05 — SBX-04 / SBX-05 clarifications only if the manifest-vs-load-report ruling changes their wording; dev-guide — none expected (DG-KRN-JOB / TXN-08 already govern). 

**Tests.** BUILD_SPEC acceptance (DB-bound, authored NOT RUN): `tests/domain/platform/test_sandbox_load.py` — `test_sandbox_copy_k03` (▶ dependency: `worlds.k03_castellan` does not exist in `tests/support/worlds.py` at 9e7d1031; the K-03 world after CLO-7's re-lock is another lane's fixture — SNP-2 binds to it when it exists and, until then, authors the case against a lane-built minimal world with the same assertions, stating the substitution), `test_restore_into_new_sandbox`, `test_snapshot_loaded_audit_event`, `test_determinism_mismatch_warning`, `test_memberships_copied_not_sessions`; `tests/pg/test_sandbox_isolation.py::test_sandbox_session_cannot_write_source`. CPU witnesses (run in the lane): the pure load planner (dataset order, digest and stamp verification, `tenant_id` re-stamping, `head_stream_version` check, NULL handling of rebuild steps), the actor / visibility rule (a SYSTEM principal is refused before any write; the target write runs as the requester), the refusal catalogue, the replay plan → command mapping, the determinism report and the load-report / audit-detail shapes; DG-ARC-08 (`load_sandbox` reached only through the job handler; the route never imports the handler), DG-ARC-14, PRV-01 and audit-catalogue completeness.

**Sequence after the ruling:** docs first (04 rows above; numbers on request) → `sandboxes.py` + the job's load-only mode + the route + OpenAPI → CPU witnesses → gates on slot 2 only (release on every exit; live owner pid) → record → READY naming head, tree, gates and pre-check basis. Integrated batch #6 on 9e7d1031 runs until ≈10:05; no landing before its END.

### 14.3 Rulings applied (D-98 candidate 137, amendments 1 and 2), docs first, code, witnesses

**Rulings (supervisor, engineering):** (1) load-only mode of `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` for restores — accepted; API-S-Job surfaces `mode` derived from params; (2) in-job sequential recompute, one transaction per group, FAILS by name when the budget is exceeded (never a partial success) — the RCP-19 fan-out is the named follow-up; (3) a refused replay transition = WARNING `SANDBOX_REPLAY_BLOCKED`, the period at its last reachable state, the load SUCCEEDED, `blocked_periods` in the load report and in `tenant.snapshot_loaded`, and every consumer requiring point-in-time equivalence (GPB-3 / GK-06 replay) refuses by name while it is non-empty; (4) ONE load report for `derived_mismatches` for both entry points (the D-98 132 pattern: the audit event is the retained link; no T-PLT-34 column, no 0068; the export manifest not extended); (5) numbers 04 1.67, 05 1.20; **amendment 1** — no new §15.2 slugs (§15.2 rule 1 composes from 02-PRD §5.5, `PROBLEMS` pinned 49, DG-ARC-13 copy, `EREV-SBX-001` already `sandbox-restricted`): the five load refusals are rule_ids on existing slugs — `SNAPSHOT_NOT_LOADABLE` / `SNAPSHOT_REGISTRY_MISMATCH` / `SNAPSHOT_DIGEST_MISMATCH` on 412 `precondition-failed`, `SANDBOX_ACTOR_REQUIRED` on 403 `forbidden`, `SANDBOX_NAME_TAKEN` on 422 `validation-failed` (field `name`), the DB-15 refusal = `sandbox-restricted`; E-68 `AUDIT_DIGEST` widened to job-written verification artefacts (the load report inherits the audit-digest retention — conservative, stated); audit actions `tenant.sandbox_requested` (AUD-CMD `tenant_sandboxes_request`), `tenant.snapshot_loaded` (sandbox first event), `tenant.sandbox_restored` (source, on completion); **amendment 2** — 02-PRD **1.13** with IMP-116 `SANDBOX_REPLAY_BLOCKED` (DG-ARC-13: every §15.4 code needs a finding-copy row; the IMP pin 115 → 116 in the same commit). k03: `worlds.k03_castellan` is F-CLO's K03 / WLD-X-13 world, not landed — the case is authored against a lane-built minimal world with the same assertion shape, the substitution named in the module docstring and here; re-pointed when F-CLO lands it.

### 14.5 Codex production-20260921-1623 §1 (relayed by the supervisor; D-98 137 AMENDMENT 3): SNP2-PERIOD-1 / REPLAY-2 / LOCK-3 at 223a9c59 — READY at 223a9c59 NOT accepted; corrected on the same line — 00bc2905

**Returned (report `PRODUCTION-FSNP-SNP2-PERIOD-REPLAY-223a9c59.md`, SHA-256 `e844d76f…`; three SEQUENTIAL source defects, none observed in a run):** PERIOD-1 — `sandboxes.py:800–807` and `:866–873` called `db.transitions.apply(..., "period_state", ...)`, but the DB-03 / IM-S registry has no `period_state` entry (`apply:1097` indexes `TRANSITIONS[table]` → KeyError on the first non-empty load), and a registry entry would not have been the repair: DB-07 (`0029:89–97`) requires the matching transition row inserted in the SAME transaction BEFORE the state update, which the loader's initialisation (NULL → future, then an update to open without a future → open row) and its replay (update before insert) both violated; the governed owner is the reference / close workflow (`reference.commands.open_period`, `close.commands._record_transition`, `_persist_lock`). REPLAY-2 — `decode_rows` yields JSON strings for UUID / date / datetime; the loader kept the REPLAY_REFERENCE rows untyped and passed them to `replay_plan`, whose `_uuid` / `_aware` require typed objects, so every ordinary non-empty export would have failed there — mislabelled a registry mismatch. LOCK-3 — every non-transition step was skipped while the comment claimed locks were written below: no `period_lock` row, `period_lock_id` omitted, `current_lock_id` never advanced, `verify_end_state` never called. §14.3's interpretation ("the DB-03 kernel, not the HTTP close commands") is WITHDRAWN: the ruling is the governed mutable-period path composed through the normal commands with their locks, permissions and `period_machine.decide` guards — a private writer proves none of those controls.

**Correction — 00bc2905** (`make fmt` rc=0; `make typecheck` rc=0 `typecheck-73.log` after one `dict` type-argument fix (`typecheck-72.log` rc=2); lane CPU set rc=0 — **278 passed** (`pytest-118-lane/rc.txt`: the 274 of §14.3 minus `tests/domain/platform/test_registry_resolve.py` (a DB-fixture module: its run in `pytest-117-lane` showed the 5 DB-fixture errors "database erev_rv_l23_test does not exist" beside 280 passed) plus the 5 new CPU guards); the sandbox modules + architecture guards rc=0, 109 passed (`pytest-116/rc.txt`); both DB modules and the pg module collect (8 tests); `make lint` rc=0 (`lint-149.log`, the commit gate)):
1. **NEW `erev_api/domain/platform/sandbox_periods.py` — the period replay executor.** `prepare(references)` (pure): `snapshot_replay.replay_plan` over TYPED rows, `verify_end_state` against the exported `period_state` rows (a plan that does not reproduce the exported end state is refused by name), and `pair_steps` — every transition to `closed` / `reopened` / `permanently_locked` must be immediately followed by its lock step (a lock without its transition or a transition to a locked state without its lock is refused by name) — all BEFORE the recompute and before any period write. `execute(prepared, runtime, actor, …)` (DB): Phase A creates every source `period_state` as `future` with its SOURCE id and the plan's NULL → future transitions with their SOURCE ids (the `_sync_period_states` shape; one AUD-FACT), then one transaction per paired step (TXN-08): `future → open` as `open_period` does (API-R-18 `authorize`, the state, the kept book, then `close.commands._record_transition(transition_id=step.id)`); `→ closing` / `closing → open` as `start_close` / `cancel_close` do (`require_for_entity(period.close)`, the allowed pair, the cancel reason subset); `closing → closed` + LOCK as `_period_lock_approved` does — the thirteen gates evaluated IN THE SANDBOX, `period_machine.decide(LOCK, Guards(comment, gate_results, requester = the copied request's preparer, approver = the copied APPROVE decision's approver, mfa_verified_at = the decision's recorded MFA time, now = the decision time))`, the producers resolved by name, the datasets frozen, the relock diff (CLO-7), `_persist_lock(lock_id = the SOURCE lock id, transition_id = the SOURCE transition id)` — lock row first (the 0047 deferred key, D-98 61) — then the lock snapshots, the stored gate results, the certified reconciliations, CTL-016, NTF-06 and the audit; `closed → reopened` + REOPEN as `_period_reopen_approved` does (`approvals.decisions_of` over the COPIED decisions as of the decision time, quorum and self-approval, BR-CLS-05 on the sandbox's state, `_persist_lock(REOPEN)`, reconciliations reopened, NTF-07, audit); `closed → permanently_locked` as `_execute_permanent_lock` does (the decision, the permanent-lock order). The source lock's `previous_lock_id` must equal the sandbox's current lock (an identity the replay preserved earlier) — a difference is a DEFECT and raises. BR-CLS-03 is not re-run: the source's own `future → open` of the next period is a step of the plan. After the plan the reached `(state, current_lock_id)` of every unblocked period must equal the source's — a difference raises. Timestamps and the acting principal are the load's (`created_at = uow.now`; the requester), the identities the source's.
2. **The acting principal.** `sandboxes.sandbox_actor(sandbox, requested_by, at)`: the requesting user as a principal OF THE SANDBOX — their COPIED ACTIVE membership and `effective_grants` of its copied assignments (the `_session_context` shape of the auth kernel; `auth_method="system"`, no MFA of their own). Their permissions are the guards' facts: a requester without `period.close` is refused by the normal command and the period is BLOCKED by name — the SYSTEM principal was not given permissions it does not hold. `require_for_entity(period.close)` is required for every replayed step (the close workflow's permission; stated as the interpretation).
3. **Refusal classification (`blocked_reason`).** A `Problem` (slug, rule ids, detail) or the database's own named guard (SQLSTATE P0001 with an `EREV-` message: DB-07 `EREV-PER-001`, the posting guard) → the period is BLOCKED at its last reachable state, its later steps skipped, `SANDBOX_REPLAY_BLOCKED` raised (ruling (3)). A foreign-key violation, a KeyError, a ValueError, a RuntimeError → PROPAGATE (the job fails by its traceback): never disguised. An inconsistent export (`ValueError` at `prepare`) is `SNAPSHOT_NOT_LOADABLE` "the replay reference datasets are inconsistent: …" — no longer "registry mismatch".

The registry guard pins that the root and its dependants are `LOAD_ORDER` datasets (dependants after the root) naming real columns and that every `FINALIZATIONS` table belongs to a graph — a finalized row is restored with its whole graph, never table by table.
2. **The loader's copy loop** — `read_rows` (the stored file opened in the SOURCE context, decoded, verified, counted) and `insert_row` (typed, re-stamped, deferred references held, a finalized row inserted in its initial status) factored out; when `LOAD_ORDER` reaches a graph root, `load_graphs` reads the root's dependants with it (verified and counted like any dataset), types every row, orders the graphs with the pure `sandboxes.graph_batches` (a dependant naming no root is an inconsistent export → `SNAPSHOT_NOT_LOADABLE`), and loads ONE graph per transaction: the request (PENDING if finalized), its steps in `step_no` order (ACTIVE if they were), its decisions in `decided_at` order under the LIVE 0013 guard, then this graph's `FINALIZATIONS` through the DB-03 kernel (steps before the request) — before the next root's graph inserts. `submitted_at` order guarantees no two requests of one subject are PENDING together (a completed request was decided before the next of the same subject was submitted — the index enforced that in the source) and puts a legitimately current PENDING request last. The dependant datasets are skipped when `LOAD_ORDER` reaches them (already loaded with their root); a root dataset without rows admits no dependant rows. The generic `finalize_after` stays for a `FINALIZATIONS` table outside a graph (none today).

4. **CPU guards** (`tests/unit/test_sandbox_finalization.py`): `test_graphs_name_real_columns_and_load_order`; `test_graph_batches_restore_one_subject_s_requests_in_submission_order` — the lock, the re-lock and a current PENDING request of one period state come out in `submitted_at` order with their own steps and decisions (decisions by `decided_at`), the PENDING request last, an orphan decision refused by name.

Ruling: the `FINALIZATIONS` pattern applied to memberships — copied membership rows whose FINAL status is not ACTIVE load in their activation state, the approval graphs load under the live guards, then each membership's later status is applied with the original evidence inside the same load; final state, ids, hashes, MFA and times preserved; no permanent reactivation, no guard change, no omitted or re-approved decision. (3) WITNESS-3 residual — after building the world and the chain, ADVANCE the shared injected runtime `FrozenClock` to the cutoff before dispatch so `snapshot_job._check_row` admits `known_at ≤ now`; stamping the queued job is not enough; the production future-cutoff refusal stays witnessed separately.

1. **MEMBERSHIP-1 — `FINALIZATIONS["tenant_membership"]`** (`initial="ACTIVE"`, `final={SUSPENDED, REMOVED}`, `held=("removed_at",)`, `after="approval_decision"`, `when="activated_at"`, **`kernel=False`**): a copied membership that ended SUSPENDED or REMOVED after having been ACTIVE is inserted ACTIVE with `removed_at` held; a never-activated membership (INVITED → REMOVED) and an ACTIVE one load as exported; the approval graphs then load under the live 0013 guard (the approver's membership IS active at that moment, as it was in the source when they decided); once the `approval_decision` dataset has loaded — the graphs' datasets now trigger `finalize_after` for the held rows of OTHER tables — the membership's final status and `removed_at` are applied. **The kernel has no `tenant_membership` entry** (the DB-03 registry does not govern the membership status: T-PLT-07 is an SC-M status the platform domain writes with a plain UPDATE, `users._set_status`), so the ruling's "through the DB-03 kernel" is executed as that domain's UPDATE shape — `status` and the held columns by id under the sandbox context, with the exported `updated_at` / `updated_by` / `row_version` copied as they are (they describe the final state; no re-stamp) — `Finalization.kernel=False` names the exception explicitly and the registry guard pins that only `tenant_membership` uses it; registering `tenant_membership` in the DB-03 kernel is a kernel / 04 decision named for the supervisor, not taken here. Never a permanent reactivation: a load that fails between the graph and the finalization fails the job by name with the sandbox unusable, never SUCCEEDED with an active suspended member.
2. **The explicit partition — `Graph.current = ("status", "PENDING")`, `graph_batches`:** every FINALIZED root of the dataset in `(submitted_at, id)` order, then the legitimately CURRENT root last regardless of its `submitted_at`; equal `submitted_at` values fall back to the id (deterministic). CPU guards: `test_hold_finalization_holds_a_suspended_or_removed_membership`, the registry guard's non-kernel branch, the partition ties.

The partition (`Graph.current`: pending last regardless of `submitted_at`, ties by id) and the `_copy` clock advance are accepted as ruled. Nothing further owed before READY.

### 14.13 Codex production-20260921-1824 §1 on e4f67680 (relayed by the supervisor; D-98 137 AMENDMENT 7): SNP2-MEMBERSHIP-STAMPS-1 + SNP2-MEMBERSHIP-REFUSAL-1 — corrected on the same line — docs in place ebe012b0, code 9e1448cc

**Credited:** the finalized-before-PENDING partition and tie order, WITNESS-3 SOURCE-CLOSED; the `kernel=False` exception accepted. **Clarified (Codex 1834 §2, accepted by the supervisor):** moving only the successful restoration UPDATE into the last graph's transaction is insufficient while the temporary ACTIVE membership was committed earlier — a `read_rows` digest refusal or a graph-parsing refusal can occur before that transaction begins; acceptable shapes: (a) the held final statuses applied on EVERY supported refusal exit after the membership load, the early digest / parse / not_loadable exits included (finally-style, the refusal preserved), or (b) the whole temporary-status lifetime inside one boundary; witness an early refusal (before any graph) AND a late one (between graphs). **This lane's tail is shape (a) and runs on the early exits too:** the `try … except Problem` encloses the ENTIRE copy loop — every `read_rows` (digest mismatch, missing file), every `load_graphs` (`graph_batches` inconsistency → `SNAPSHOT_NOT_LOADABLE`), every graph transaction (a kernel `invalid-transition` at a finalization) and every plain dataset — from the first dataset on; `settle_memberships` applies only held rows whose insert committed. **Returned:** (1) STAMPS-1 — `tenant_membership` is IM-M: DB-02 `tg_touch` sets `updated_at = now()` and `row_version = OLD + 1` on the non-kernel finalization UPDATE, so §14.11's "exported `updated_*` and `row_version` kept" could not hold and the all-columns witness would have exposed it. Ruling: no trigger bypass; state DURABLY (design note + the 05 SBX sentence of the unlanded 1.20 row, in place) that a restored membership row equals the source on every column EXCEPT `tenant_id`, `updated_at` and `row_version` — the last two the sandbox's own DB-02 stamps (`row_version` = source + 1 on a finalized row; `updated_at` = the finalization instant); the witness excludes exactly those columns by name and asserts the two relations. (2) REFUSAL-1 — the temporarily-ACTIVE membership inserts commit per dataset while the finalization ran only after ALL graphs succeeded, so a handled refusal in between (digest mismatch, missing file, inconsistent graph) left a copied SUSPENDED / REMOVED member ACTIVE. Ruling: every supported refusal exit after the membership load applies the held final statuses before returning the ORIGINAL refusal (finally-style), or the finalization moves into the last graph's transaction; witness by injecting a refusal after the membership load and before the graph finalization.

**Design note (durable statement; the 05 sentence follows it):** the loader restores a guarded copied row in its INITIAL status and finalizes it after its dependants — through the DB-03 kernel where the kernel governs the table, through the platform domain's own status UPDATE for `tenant_membership` (the live write path's shape, so restoration fidelity equals production — supervisor 137-A6 addendum). Because `tenant_membership` is IM-M, that UPDATE receives the sandbox's own DB-02 stamps; a restored membership therefore equals the source on every column except `tenant_id` (remapped), `updated_at` (the finalization instant) and `row_version` (source + 1) — never a bypassed trigger. Rows that needed no finalization (ACTIVE, INVITED, never-activated) keep the exported stamps exactly. A supported refusal after the membership load settles the held final statuses in a fresh transaction before the refusal returns, so no copied member is ever ACTIVE behind a refused load; a held row whose insert never committed matches nothing (`WHERE status = initial`) and is dropped.

**Docs in place — ebe012b0** (`make lint` rc=0 `lint-155.log`, the governed-document guards in the same condition): `docs/05-ARCHITECTURE.md` rev 1.20 SBX-04 (this lane's unlanded row) gains the sentence above (guarded copied rows restored in their initial status and finalized after their dependants; the membership stamps; the refusal tail); the 1.20 log row and the header cell name the in-place amendment; no new number (the supervisor's "in place"). §14.11's claim "the exported stamps kept" is CORRECTED here.
